<a id="browser-extension"></a>

## Browser extension

**Key:** `browser-extension` · **Starter today:** absent · **Open rows:** 24

The reference ships a complete MV3 browser-extension package (`/Users/rico/projects/trackyourtime/packages/extension`) with a generated-per-target manifest, a service-worker background whose entire state is a rebuildable cache, a popup that paints from a versioned session-scoped snapshot, a page↔extension sign-in handoff over `externally_connectable`, a second (Gecko) engine target, server-side trust for both pinned and random-per-install extension origins, an id-derivation module shared by the dev server and the CLI printer, and a two-store release workflow that fails closed. The Hatchkit starter has nothing for this area: `starter/packages` holds only `client`, `server`, `shared`; `starter/.github/workflows` has only `build-and-deploy.yml`, `desktop-release.yml`, `mobile-release.yml`; and a repo-wide grep for "browser extension", "chrome web store", "manifest v3" and "web-ext" across `docs/`, `cli/src/` and `starter/` returns nothing. The CLI's `Feature` union (`cli/src/prompts.ts:82`) has no extension member, and `cli/src/scaffold/native-origins.ts:29` lists only `mobile`, `desktop`, `desktop-tauri` as origin-bearing clients. The only traces are forward-looking: `cli/src/scaffold/identifiers.ts:56` already reserves an `extension` client-id slot, and `cli/src/deploy/trusted-origins.ts:35` cites a hand-added `chrome-extension://<id>` as the reason its origin merge must never overwrite.

### Gaps

- [ ] **Add a browser-extension package as a scaffoldable feature**
  - The starter has no extension workspace at all, and the CLI's feature union cannot express one. The feature must add a package holding a background worker entry, a popup document, a storage/config layer, a build config that emits the manifest, unit tests with a faked browser API surface, and root scripts per build target — plus registration in the workspace globs and the aggregate test script. It must go through the existing additive/idempotent feature contract so `update` can add it to an already-scaffolded repo.
  - *Invariant:* A surface that is not in the feature union cannot be added by `update` later, and a package that is not in the workspace globs and the aggregate test script builds locally and is never type-checked or tested in CI.
  - *Reference:* `packages/extension/ (whole package); package.json:27-40; pnpm-workspace.yaml; cli/src/prompts.ts:82; cli/src/features/contract.ts:1-52`
- [ ] **Generate the manifest per build target from committed code, never ship a static one**
  - Build targets are declared as a typed table in a committed source file — one entry per (backend origin, display name, allowed host origins, pinned key, output directory, engine) combination — and the bundler emits the manifest from it at build time. Target-varying values are injected into the bundle as build-time defines from the same table, so the manifest and the runtime check can never name two different lists.
  - *Invariant:* A static manifest file can only ever describe one target, and target values kept in dotfiles that are conventionally gitignored produce a fresh clone that builds a bundle with an empty origin and no error to say so.
  - *Reference:* `packages/extension/manifest.config.ts:1-24, 94-172; packages/extension/vite.config.ts:21-70`
- [ ] **Bake the backend origin in at build time with no fallback, and allow a runtime override**
  - The build-time origin is read from the injected define, narrowed, and throws at module scope if absent. A single store build serves everyone, so the popup exposes a picker that persists a normalized origin in the persistent storage area; the baked value is only the default. The stored value is re-validated on write and stored as a bare origin.
  - *Invariant:* A default fallback cannot know which build it is standing in for, so a production bundle whose define went missing ships pointed at a developer laptop and presents as 'the extension is broken' rather than as the build error it is. Storing anything but a normalized origin lets a pasted page URL or a trailing slash become part of every request path, with no way back visible to the user.
  - *Reference:* `packages/extension/src/lib/config.ts:24-101`
- [ ] **Pin the extension id with a committed public key before the first store upload**
  - The Chromium-family id is the hash of a public key's DER bytes. Commit the public half plus the id it produces as constants in the shared package, derive nothing at runtime in the browser bundle, and have a server-side test recompute the id from the key. A build-time env override exists for forks publishing their own listing.
  - *Invariant:* The backend allowlist must be able to name the extension's origin before the store listing exists, or a server released today cannot be used by the extension a store approves next month. If the committed key and the committed id are not recomputed against each other by a test they drift silently, and every request from the shipped extension is refused.
  - *Reference:* `packages/shared/src/store-clients.ts:18-38; packages/extension/src/manifest.test.ts:31-44; CLAUDE.md:770-776`
- [ ] **Derive the unpacked-load id in dev tooling and trust it automatically**
  - One module implements both forms of id derivation — from a pinned key, and from the absolute load path of the unpacked build directory — and is imported by both the dev server (which appends the derived origin to the server's trusted-origin list) and the command that prints the origin for any other backend. The dev server also trusts the pinned store id so a production-target unpacked build can be pointed at a local backend. The output directory name is itself frozen, because the unpacked id is a hash of that path.
  - *Invariant:* A dev backend that trusts a different id than the browser assigns fails as a flat origin-refusal with nothing on either side saying the two disagreed. If the printer and the truster derive the id separately they can disagree; moving the output directory silently changes the id and invalidates every already-listed dev origin.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/lib/extension-id.mjs:1-64; scripts/dev.mjs:292-315; packages/extension/manifest.config.ts:110-114`
- [ ] **Declare a permission floor and assert it in a test**
  - No host permissions, no optional host permissions, no cookie permission; the permission list is exactly the small set the worker needs (persistent storage, a scheduler, an idle signal). Anything wider is an optional permission requested from the user gesture that enables the feature needing it. A unit test asserts the exact list and the absence of the forbidden keys, on every target.
  - *Invariant:* Without host permissions every request the extension makes is an ordinary cross-origin request, so the backend must trust its origin for ALL traffic, not only sign-in — a fact that is invisible until something other than sign-in fails. A permission added 'just for one fetch' becomes an install warning on every store user's machine, and a store review decision that lives only in prose is re-litigated by the next contributor.
  - *Reference:* `packages/extension/manifest.config.ts:266-273; packages/extension/src/manifest.test.ts:11-18; CLAUDE.md:2699-2707`
- [ ] **Register every background listener synchronously at module scope**
  - The service-worker entry registers all listeners at module scope, before any await. Handlers themselves may be async. The module also documents that nothing may log the credential or an auth response, because the worker console is readable from the browser's extension page.
  - *Invariant:* The runtime revives an evicted worker by re-evaluating the module and THEN delivering the event that woke it. A listener added after an await is registered after that delivery, so the first message on a cold start — which is most of them — is dropped, and the surface silently does nothing.
  - *Reference:* `packages/extension/src/background/index.ts:1-14, 619-650`
- [ ] **Do periodic work on the platform alarm scheduler, and never re-create a live alarm**
  - Recurring upkeep runs from a named alarm at the platform's minimum period, created only when one does not already exist. The alarm handler is the single place a permanently-down socket is reconnected, a stuck outbound queue is flushed, and a missed idle transition is re-polled, because each of those otherwise depends on an in-memory timer.
  - *Invariant:* An in-memory timer dies with the evicted worker and the surface freezes at whatever it last showed; the alarm is the only scheduler that revives a dead worker. Re-creating the alarm on every wake pushes the next fire another full period out each time, so a busy worker's upkeep can stall indefinitely. The runtime only reports idle TRANSITIONS, so one missed while the worker was gone is never acted on unless it is re-polled.
  - *Reference:* `packages/extension/src/background/index.ts:117-175, 672-683`
- [ ] **Treat all worker state as a cache rebuilt from storage, with one recovery path**
  - Session, API client, outbound queue and live socket are module-level caches whose cold-start value is empty. One `ensureReady` rebuilds all of them from storage alone, and every entry point awaits it. One `bootstrap` runs on install, on browser start, and on plain module evaluation — which is what an eviction-and-revival looks like from inside — and all three do the same thing because the worker cannot tell them apart. Bootstrap swallows its own failure; the next alarm retries.
  - *Invariant:* The runtime evicts an idle worker after about thirty seconds, so nothing in it may be KEPT, only cached. A value held across an eviction reads as present on a warm worker and absent on a cold one, which is the same bug appearing intermittently. A cold start with no network must still leave the worker able to answer the UI.
  - *Reference:* `packages/extension/src/background/runtime.ts:1-12; packages/extension/src/background/index.ts:684-720`
- [ ] **Split extension storage by durability: credential in the memory-only area, durable data in the persistent one**
  - The storage adapter exposes both areas behind the same key-value interface the shared client runtime already consumes. The credential and anything derived from it live in the memory-only area so they never touch disk and die with the browser process; the chosen backend origin, the outbound queue and long-lived local data live in the persistent area. Every read and write is wrapped so a quota error or a torn-down area cannot take down the UI. A malformed stored record reads as 'signed out', never as an error.
  - *Invariant:* A credential moved to the persistent area is a credential written to disk on every user's machine. Conversely, the outbound queue in the memory-only area loses work the user believes was saved. Throwing on a malformed record blanks the surface, when the only recovery was signing in again anyway.
  - *Reference:* `packages/extension/src/lib/chrome-storage.ts:1-67; packages/extension/src/lib/session.ts:1-12, 52-81`
- [ ] **Paint the popup from a versioned snapshot in the memory-only area**
  - The worker writes its last full response to a versioned record in the memory-only storage area. The popup renders that copy first and replaces it when the fresh response lands. Every action still goes through the worker, which acts on the backend's truth. The record carries a schema version and is discarded when it does not match.
  - *Invariant:* The popup document is destroyed on every close, so most opens wake a cold worker that must re-read everything before it can answer — seconds of a bare loading state for data the user saw a minute earlier. The snapshot must live in the memory-only area so it never outlives the account it describes, and must be version-tagged or an older build's shape renders as a crash.
  - *Reference:* `packages/extension/src/lib/popup-snapshot.ts:1-60`
- [ ] **One message channel: request in, whole state snapshot out**
  - The worker owns everything stateful; the UI is a renderer that sends a message and gets a complete state snapshot back, never a patch. Every successful mutation returns the fresh snapshot built after the write landed. The message listener returns the keep-port-open signal so the async reply can be delivered. An authorization failure raised by a MUTATION drops the dead credential and returns the signed-out snapshot, not just the failure.
  - *Invariant:* The UI document is rebuilt on every open, so a half-applied patch is indistinguishable from a stale one. Without the keep-port-open return the platform closes the port when the listener returns and every call hangs until it times out. If only the read path handles a rejected credential, a mutation's rejection leaves the dead credential in storage and the UI rendering as signed in, with every further press failing the same way.
  - *Reference:* `packages/extension/src/lib/messaging.ts:1-57; packages/extension/src/background/index.ts:600-635`
- [ ] **Page-to-extension sign-in handoff: page drives, no credential crosses, sender checked three ways**
  - The extension lists the first-party web origins under the connectable key and the page messages it by pinned id. Every exchange is one request from the page and one reply; the extension never initiates. The page sends its session state; the extension replies with what the page should do — nothing, approve a pending device authorization it started, or sign out. The extension exchanges the approved device code for its OWN credential and adopts it only after a session lookup names the expected user. Every sender is checked against the build's target list, by messenger shape (a top-level, non-incognito, non-extension sender), and by equality with the backend's declared web origin; the page likewise refuses to run in a frame. Messages arrive on the external listener only, never the internal one. A message never changes the backend, the scope or a setting.
  - *Invariant:* The reply's sender is the only trust anchor for the short-lived approval code — a code taken from a URL, a postMessage or storage does not reveal which client it belongs to. A pending or failed session lookup sent as 'nobody is signed in' signs the extension out whenever the page is offline. A cross-site frame gets no same-site cookie, so its honest 'nobody is signed in' would sign a linked extension out; both ends must refuse frames, and the host must send frame-ancestors none. An explicitly established local session (password, device) must ignore the page's sign-in, sign-out and account switches, or a deliberate local sign-in is displaced by whoever is signed in on the page. Every extension sign-out must revoke the extension's OWN session row, or a long-lived session is parked in the account's device list forever.
  - *Reference:* `packages/shared/src/extension-bridge.ts:1-115; CLAUDE.md:2815-2890; packages/extension/src/background/bridge.ts; packages/extension/src/lib/sign-out-marker.ts`
- [ ] **Sign-out marker: same user, same backend, newer than the page session, once, with a TTL**
  - An explicit sign-out in the extension writes a marker `{ userId, backendOrigin, at }` to persistent storage. The page's next sync is answered with 'sign out' only when the user matches, the backend origin matches, the marker is newer than the page session's creation time, and the marker is younger than its TTL. A page sign-out deletes the marker. The extension has no content script, so the page acts on its next mount, focus or visibility change, not instantly.
  - *Invariant:* Without the creation-time check the marker signs the user out again every time they sign back in on the page. Without the TTL (set to the page session lifetime, so no session predating the sign-out can still be alive) the marker outlives every session it could legitimately end. Without the user and origin checks it signs out a different person, or the same person on a different backend.
  - *Reference:* `packages/shared/src/extension-bridge.ts:44-52; CLAUDE.md:2885-2900`
- [ ] **Second engine target: different background shape, permanent add-on id, no bridge key**
  - The same source builds for a second browser engine under its own mode and output directory. That engine's background is an event page, not a service worker, so the manifest carries a scripts array rather than a worker entry. It needs a permanent add-on id and a minimum-version floor driven by whichever manifest key the store requires. It supports neither the id-pinning key nor page-to-extension messaging, so both are omitted entirely — and the bundle's injected target value is an explicit 'no bridge' rather than an absent one. Tests assert each difference.
  - *Invariant:* A manifest carrying the wrong background key loads with NO background at all — every listener unregistered, and a UI that opens and does nothing. The add-on id is permanent once listed: the store keys the listing on it and the browser keys the profile's stored data on it, so changing it orphans every install's local queue and settings. An empty allowed-origin list is not a narrower bridge, it is no bridge — so a build with no listed origins must refuse every sender explicitly rather than fall through to the development list. A stray id-pinning key in a manifest the other store reviews reads as a build somebody forgot to clean up.
  - *Reference:* `packages/extension/manifest.config.ts:68-92, 139-171, 249-280; packages/extension/src/manifest.test.ts:66-120; CLAUDE.md:2721-2765`
- [ ] **Backend trust for extension origins that are random per install**
  - Engines that assign each install a fresh UUID origin cannot be listed in an allowlist. The backend trusts those origins by SHAPE — scheme plus a UUID and nothing else — narrowed by two conditions: the request carries no session cookie, and the response never carries the allow-credentials header. It is off unless the operator opts in. ONE function answers for the auth library's trusted-origin check, the CORS delegate, the health endpoint's self-report, and the socket upgrade. The derivable-id scheme is deliberately excluded from the shape rule: those are trusted by exact origin or not at all.
  - *Invariant:* Key the cookie test on the SESSION cookie, never on 'any cookie': a socket upgrade cannot opt out of cookies, and a cross-site-eligible CDN cookie rides along on every upgrade from an extension origin, so 'any cookie' silently kills the socket for everyone behind such a CDN. The session cookie is same-site and never reaches a cross-site extension origin, so its presence means the request is not the bearer path it claims to be. The socket upgrade is where this matters most — sockets are not subject to cross-origin checks, so without the rule any add-on in the browser could open one on the signed-in person's cookie. Four call sites answering separately about one request is four chances to disagree.
  - *Reference:* `packages/server/src/auth/extension-origins.ts:1-95; packages/shared/src/store-clients.ts:59-90; docs/firefox-extension-spike.md`
- [ ] **Never read a refused cross-origin request as a refusal by the application**
  - With no host permissions, a request the browser refuses for origin reasons reaches the fetch caller as an ordinary network TypeError — the same shape as being offline. So a transport failure makes the worker re-probe the backend's health endpoint (rate-limited to at most once a minute; that endpoint must answer with a wildcard allow-origin so it still gets through), a negative trust report in the snapshot renders a dedicated not-trusted notice naming the exact setting or allowlist entry to add, and the outbound queue keeps every row.
  - *Invariant:* Treat the TypeError as a refusal and a real outage drops the user's unsent work. Treat it as plain offline and the queue waits forever behind a connection that is fine. The notice must name the setting for the engine in question — for a random-UUID origin the allowlist cannot hold the value at all, so naming the allowlist is a dead end for the user.
  - *Reference:* `CLAUDE.md:2782-2792; packages/extension/src/popup/origin-not-trusted-notice.tsx; packages/extension/src/popup/errors.ts`
- [ ] **One version source, checked against the tag before anything is uploaded**
  - The manifest version comes from the root package version — the one number the release process bumps. A prerelease string that the store's integer-only version field cannot hold becomes the numeric prefix plus a separate display-version field. CI recomputes the expected version from the tag and fails BEFORE any upload when the built manifest disagrees, falling back to the package version on a branch build. The same build-time value is injected into the bundle so the manifest and the code cannot name two different versions.
  - *Invariant:* A second version literal drifts from the first silently. Stores refuse an upload whose version is not higher than the published one, so a forgotten bump otherwise surfaces only at upload — after the rest of the release has already gone out.
  - *Reference:* `packages/extension/manifest.config.ts:174-202; packages/extension/vite.config.ts:63-67; .github/workflows/extension-release.yml:193-209, 346-360`
- [ ] **Store release workflow: one job per store, failing closed, artifact before any store call**
  - A tag triggers one independent job per store — separate jobs, not steps, because either store can be unconfigured, refuse or be down without that saying anything about the other. Each resolves tag and mode up front from env (never interpolated inputs), refuses a non-release tag, refuses a prerelease submission, and treats half a credential pair as an error on every event while a tag run with NO credentials downgrades to artifact-only and stays green with a notice. The exact bytes are uploaded as a workflow artifact before any store call. A manual dispatch offers build / upload-draft / publish modes and a rollout percentage. Concurrency is serialized and never cancelled mid-flight.
  - *Invariant:* Half a credential set skipped instead of failed hides the missing half behind a green run. A cancelled upload can leave state the next run trips on. Without the artifact taken before the store call, a failed submission loses the exact bytes that were sent. A prerelease submitted to a store claims a version number permanently, and the store refuses a version it has already seen.
  - *Reference:* `.github/workflows/extension-release.yml:1-165, 210-238, 252-325`
- [ ] **Packaging step: strip the pinning key after asserting it pins the right listing, refuse private key material**
  - The packaging command reads the BUILT manifest (not the source), checks its version, asserts the pinning key resolves to the configured listing id, removes that key from the uploaded copy, refuses forbidden paths and any file containing private key material, and zips the directory CONTENTS with the manifest at the root. The listing id is overridable by env for a fork. The other store's job additionally uploads a source archive of the exact ref so a reviewer can read bundled code.
  - *Invariant:* Uploading with the key left in, or with a fork's key, publishes over somebody else's listing or under an id no backend trusts — and neither fails loudly. Reading the source rather than the artifact answers whether a key MAY be pinned, not whether it WAS. Without the source archive, a store reviewing minified code asks for sources by mail and the version waits.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/chrome-web-store.mjs:1-103; .github/workflows/extension-release.yml:361-404`
- [ ] **Two i18n layers, and first-render preferences read from a synchronous local cache**
  - Text the browser itself renders — the extension name and the store description — lives in the platform's own message catalog and is referenced from the manifest by message key, so it follows the BROWSER's UI language. Text inside the extension's own document uses the app's catalogs and follows the account's language preference. Theme and language are applied before the first render from a synchronous local copy, and the media-query fallback is guarded on an explicit choice being absent.
  - *Invariant:* The platform catalog resolves at install time in the browser's language and can never be changed by the user's account preference, so using it for in-app text shows one language beside another with nothing in the UI able to fix it. Because the UI document is rebuilt on every open, learning the theme or language from the worker's answer flashes the wrong one several times a day. Once a platform catalog directory exists, the default-locale key is mandatory or the extension refuses to load.
  - *Reference:* `packages/extension/src/i18n/index.ts:1-60; packages/extension/public/_locales/en/messages.json:1-14; packages/extension/manifest.config.ts:243-246; packages/extension/src/popup/theme.ts`
- [ ] **Pin the bundler's entry filenames, use a relative base, hash only shared chunks**
  - Entry filenames are stable literals because the manifest references them as strings and cannot read a build manifest. The base is relative so the emitted document's asset references resolve wherever the page is loaded from. Shared chunks are hashed under their own directory. The bundle targets a modern runtime because the background is loaded as a real module.
  - *Invariant:* Hashed entry names break the background registration on every rebuild. A default absolute base only resolves because the extension root happens to be the URL root — it breaks the moment a document is emitted in a subdirectory. Unhashed shared chunks are named after whichever module landed in them first, which can produce a root-level file that reads like an entry point and collides outright with a future entry of that name.
  - *Reference:* `packages/extension/vite.config.ts:45-96`
- [ ] **Optional-permission local capture: request from the enabling gesture, keep it on the device, scope it per account**
  - Any background capture of the user's browsing context is off by default and asks for its optional permission from the click that enables it. The capture modules import no network or API code and reach shared logic only through a narrow subpath, with a test that walks the real import graph. Everything captured is keyed by account and scope; entering a new account's scope deletes every OTHER account's rows but keeps the same person's other scopes, and forgetting the session deletes all of it. Exclusion rules apply retroactively: a record is written under the settings current when it CLOSES, and adding an exclusion purges stored records matching it. Nothing leaves the device until the user accepts a proposed record, which then travels the ordinary create path.
  - *Invariant:* Requesting the permission at install shows every store user a browsing-history warning they never asked for. If the capture modules can reach the API client, local-only capture silently becomes a network feature the first time someone imports the barrel. If a new scope wipes the same person's other scopes, switching away and back loses their rules. If an exclusion is not retroactive, excluding something the user is looking at still stores it.
  - *Reference:* `CLAUDE.md:2646-2686; packages/extension/src/background/activity/; packages/extension/src/background/activity-session.test.ts`
- [ ] **Ship the deployed-backend trust step as a release prerequisite, not a sign-in detail**
  - The deployed backend must carry the pinned extension origin in its trusted-origin list (or the opt-in flag for the random-origin scheme) BEFORE any store release reaches users. That value is set by hand in the hosting dashboard, deliberately. The release runbook states it, gives the exact check, and warns that a command-line client is a misleading way to test it.
  - *Invariant:* Without the trust the store build cannot make a single request — not one, because it has no host permissions, so this is not merely a sign-in problem. A production trust list a script can extend is a trust list nobody reviews. A command-line request sends none of the headers that force origin validation, so it succeeds against a backend a browser cannot use.
  - *Reference:* `docs/releasing.md:291-323, 461-474; CLAUDE.md:2802-2814; CLAUDE.md:1004-1013`

### Identifiers this area freezes

Every name below becomes a contract the first time this area ships. All of
them must come from the manifest's `identifiers` block — see
`cli/src/scaffold/identifiers.ts`. An area that spells one of these out as a
literal has re-introduced the divergence that block exists to prevent.

- The pinned public key for the derivable-id engine and the extension id computed from it (both committed, cross-checked by a test) — they are in every backend's trusted-origin list and in the store listing
- The store listing / item id used by the publish script
- The permanent add-on id for the random-origin engine — the store keys the listing and the browser keys the profile's stored data on it
- The extension's device-flow client id (already reserved as the `extension` slot in cli/src/scaffold/identifiers.ts:56) — allowlisted by the backend and carried by every already-issued token
- Every storage key written by the extension: the credential key, the chosen-backend-origin key, the backend-info key, the UI-snapshot key, the language key, the outbound-queue key, the sign-out-marker key, the local-capture database name — all keyed on the project's storage prefix, and all orphaning already-stored data on a rename
- Named alarm ids, since a renamed alarm leaves the old one firing for handlers that no longer exist
- The build output directory names — the unpacked-load id is a hash of the absolute path, so moving one changes the id already listed in a dev backend's trusted origins
- The bridge channel string and its protocol version number
- The platform message-catalog keys referenced from the manifest (name, dev name, description) and the default-locale code
- The request header name and per-surface value the extension sends to identify itself in the account's device list
- The env-var names that gate the two backend trust behaviours (the pinned-origin store-clients switch and the random-origin scheme switch), which appear in every self-hoster's environment and in the popup's not-trusted notice
- The CI secret names for each store's credential pair

### Domain-specific — do not extract

- The toolbar badge showing elapsed running time, and the per-30-second badge refresh being about a ticking clock — generalize to 'a periodic upkeep tick that also revives the socket, flushes the queue and re-polls idle'
- Timer start/stop, running-entry mirroring, and the idle prompt asking what to do with time spent away from the machine
- Time-entry descriptions, the server-side description autocomplete, and the Tab-completes/Enter-starts-the-timer keyboard contract of that typeahead
- Project / client / task / tag pickers, the billable flag, catalog rows and the create-then-select behaviour of catalog creation
- The activity-capture arithmetic itself: segment merging, suggestion composition from untracked stretches, clipping an accepted suggestion to what is still untracked, and subtracting a dismissal rather than matching it by range. The generic half (optional permission requested from the enabling gesture, device-local only, import-graph isolation, per-account scoping, retroactive exclusions) is extracted as a gap; this arithmetic is not
- Workspace/organization switching semantics specific to a shared timesheet — who may see whose entries, which totals are personal
- Invoicing, rates, budgets and reports surfaced anywhere in the popup
- The specific declared data-collection categories submitted to the second store (authentication info, personally identifying info, the time entries the person writes) — the MECHANISM (a store-required declaration key with its own minimum-version floor) generalizes; the list of categories is per product
- The concrete product hostnames used as the production backend origin and the production web origin in the bridge match patterns

### Reference evidence

- Per-target manifest generated from a typed build-target table in committed TypeScript (three targets: two Chromium, one Gecko), with the reason .env files are refused spelled out — `packages/extension/manifest.config.ts:1-172`
- Manifest keys assembled per engine: background as service_worker vs scripts, key present only on Chromium, externally_connectable omitted entirely when the target has no bridge, exact permission list — `packages/extension/manifest.config.ts:232-288`
- Release version read from the root package.json; prerelease split into version + version_name because the store version field is 1-4 integers — `packages/extension/manifest.config.ts:174-202`
- Permanent Gecko add-on id, strict_min_version floor, and the declared data-collection list — `packages/extension/manifest.config.ts:68-92`
- Pinned-key helper with the openssl commands to generate one and the rule that only the public half enters the repo — `packages/extension/manifest.config.ts:210-230`
- Vite build: manifest emitted by a plugin, relative base, pinned entry filenames, hashed shared chunks under chunks/, build-time defines for origin, version and bridge target — `packages/extension/vite.config.ts:28-98`
- Tests that assert the permission floor, the absence of host/cookie permissions, the per-engine background shape, the absent bridge key on Gecko, and that the two remote targets do not drift — `packages/extension/src/manifest.test.ts:11-120`
- Build-time backend origin narrowed and thrown on when missing (no fallback), runtime override persisted as a normalized origin, bridge target likewise narrowed at module scope — `packages/extension/src/lib/config.ts:24-101`
- Two storage areas with different durability: memory-only for the credential, persistent for the chosen origin and the offline queue; every read/write wrapped so a storage failure cannot take down the UI — `packages/extension/src/lib/chrome-storage.ts:1-67`
- Credential stored only in the memory-only area with an explicit 'do not move this' rule, plus a session-provenance tag (web | password | device) — `packages/extension/src/lib/session.ts:1-90`
- Versioned last-response snapshot in the memory-only area so the popup paints instantly on a cold worker; stale-while-revalidate, writes still go through the worker — `packages/extension/src/lib/popup-snapshot.ts:1-60`
- One-request/one-whole-snapshot protocol between popup and worker, with the reason partial patches are refused; view declared so the worker fetches only what is on screen — `packages/extension/src/lib/messaging.ts:1-57`
- Service-worker entry: synchronous module-scope listener registration only, async handlers, and the no-secret-logging rule — `packages/extension/src/background/index.ts:1-14`
- Periodic upkeep on a platform alarm rather than setInterval, with the guard against re-creating an existing alarm and the list of things only the alarm can revive (socket reconnect, queue flush, idle re-poll) — `packages/extension/src/background/index.ts:117-175`
- Listener block: message port kept open with `return true`, bridge listener registered at module scope, alarm dispatch, and one bootstrap shared by install, startup and plain module evaluation — `packages/extension/src/background/index.ts:619-720`
- All worker state is a cache rebuilt from storage by ensureReady, awaited by every entry point — `packages/extension/src/background/runtime.ts:1-12`
- Bridge protocol: channel constant, protocol version + supported-versions list, sync throttle, sign-out-marker TTL, per-target match patterns, and an explicit second origin check so a manifest typo cannot widen trust — `packages/shared/src/extension-bridge.ts:1-115`
- Pinned public key committed before the first upload, the derived id written out as a constant, and the note that a server test recomputes it so the two cannot drift — `packages/shared/src/store-clients.ts:18-57`
- Shape-matcher for random-per-install extension origins (moz-/safari-), deliberately excluding the derivable chromium scheme — `packages/shared/src/store-clients.ts:59-90`
- Server-side trust for random extension origins: per-scheme, narrowed by no session cookie and never Allow-Credentials, behind an opt-in flag, answered by one function for auth trust, CORS, health and socket upgrade — `packages/server/src/auth/extension-origins.ts:1-95`
- Id derivation (pinned-key form and unpacked-path form) in one module shared by the dev server and the id-printing command, with the failure it closes — `scripts/lib/extension-id.mjs:1-64`
- Dev server derives the unpacked dev id and the pinned store id, trusts both, and prints the bridge ids and the random-origin trust line — `scripts/dev.mjs:292-315, 376-409`
- Packaging step: version checked against the tag, pinning key removed after asserting it pins the right listing, forbidden paths and private-key material refused, directory contents zipped with the manifest at the root — `scripts/chrome-web-store.mjs:1-103`
- Two independent store jobs on the same tag; both fail closed on half a credential pair, downgrade to artifact-only on a tag with no credentials, refuse prereleases, upload the exact bytes as an artifact before any store call — `.github/workflows/extension-release.yml:1-238`
- Second-store job additionally builds the other engine target, re-checks the version, and uploads a git archive of the tag as reviewable sources — `.github/workflows/extension-release.yml:239-415`
- Two i18n layers: platform message catalog for browser-rendered chrome (name, description) referenced as __MSG_*__, versus the in-app catalog that follows the account's language preference — `packages/extension/src/i18n/index.ts:1-60`
- Platform message catalog with the store description's character limit recorded in the entry's own description field — `packages/extension/public/_locales/en/messages.json:1-14`

<a id="raycast-mcp"></a>

## Raycast + MCP

**Key:** `raycast-mcp` · **Starter today:** absent · **Open rows:** 27

The reference ships two separate host-integration packages: `packages/raycast` (a macOS launcher extension, 5 commands, ~3.5k lines of `src/lib` plus a generated `src/vendor/` copy of the shared client core, plus two build scripts that produce and verify a standalone store-publishable copy) and `packages/mcp` (a stdio MCP server, `@starter/mcp`, binary `trackyourtime-mcp`, that talks only to the public `/api/v1` REST surface with a scoped bearer token). Both reuse `@starter/core` / `@starter/shared` rather than reimplementing logic, and both authenticate without a cookie jar — the launcher via RFC 8628 device flow into the host's encrypted per-extension store, the MCP server via a pasted scoped API token in two env vars. The Hatchkit starter has neither package and no prerequisite for either: `starter/packages` is only `client`, `server`, `shared` — there is no framework-free client core package, no bearer/device-flow auth, no scoped API tokens, and no public REST surface (grep of `starter/packages/server/src` for bearer/device/scopes returns only unrelated Listmonk env vars). The only trace anywhere in Hatchkit is `cli/src/scaffold/identifiers.ts:56`, which already reserves `"raycast"` and `"mcp"` as client-host slots in `CLIENT_HOSTS` and therefore already mints a device-flow client id for each — the identifier contract exists, the surfaces it names do not.

It is a UI shell only: every piece of behaviour more than one client needs lives in the shared framework-free client core, and the host package holds host widgets and host storage bindings.
  - *Invariant:* Host packages are not in the main build graph, so a change that breaks them compiles green through the whole pipeline. The aggregate typecheck, lint and unit-test scripts must name every host package explicitly, and CI must run the aggregate typecheck rather than relying on the build.

A `--check` mode reports missing, stale and orphaned files and fails the build.

The vendor copy must be generated, never hand-edited, and the check must be in the unit test run — a stale copy is a silent behaviour fork between the host surface and every other client.   - *Invariant:* A bearer credential is a credential for exactly one backend. After the origin preference moves, sending the old credential hands this account's session to a server that has no business holding it, and revoking against the new origin leaves the real session alive on the old one. Both failures are silent.

Any surface re-reads it once a second — a local read, no network — and a reconciliation rule lets the echo outrank a snapshot that was FETCHED BEFORE it.

The echo needs no TTL precisely because the reconciliation compares fetch timestamps: a stale record of a stop can then never mask a state change that originated on another device. Writing the echo anywhere but the central choke point means the next command added forgets it.

The host's own "refresh this command" call is a nudge on top, never one of the four.
  - *Invariant:* The host may decline a background refresh request, and a command that is still loaded is not remounted by one — so a refresh call alone leaves the surface displaying state the user changed a second ago from another command. Also: the push socket must NOT filter this install's own origin id, because every command of one install shares that id and filtering drops exactly the event the persistent surface needs most. And polling underneath a connected socket asks a question already answered.

The one background-capable action command exists specifically because a global hotkey cannot drive a view command.

### Identifiers this area freezes

Every name below becomes a contract the first time this area ships. All of
them must come from the manifest's `identifiers` block — see
`cli/src/scaffold/identifiers.ts`. An area that spells one of these out as a
literal has re-introduced the divergence that block exists to prevent.

- Host extension manifest `name` — the host keys its encrypted per-extension key-value store by it, so a change orphans every install's stored credential, durable queue and state echo
- Host store publisher `author` handle — part of the same storage key and the published listing identity; permanent after first publish
- Device-flow client id per host surface (`<token>-raycast`, `<token>-mcp`, one per entry in CLIENT_HOSTS) — server-allowlisted and carried by every already-issued credential
- Client-identification request header name (`x-<token>-client`) — drives per-surface session labels and per-surface revocation
- Client-version request header name (`x-<token>-client-version`) and API-level header name — read by the server's compatibility floor
- Host storage keys for the credential, the account email, the account id and the issuing origin (`<token>.session.token`, `.email`, `.userId`, `.server`)
- Host storage key for the stable per-install origin id (`<token>.originId`) and the id's prefix (`raycast-<id>`) — the push feed's own-write discrimination depends on it
- Host storage key prefix for the authoritative-state echo, the durable queue, the optimistic overlay and the read cache
- MCP server name announced to the host, and its binary name (`<token>-mcp`)
- MCP environment variable names (`<ENVPREFIX>_API_TOKEN`, `<ENVPREFIX>_API_URL`) — in every user's host configuration file
- Default API origin and default web/approval origin — baked into the release build of every host package and into N other places that must agree
- API credential prefix (e.g. `tt_`) — users match on it
- npm workspace scope for the generated packages — every import specifier in the vendored copy depends on it

### Domain-specific — do not extract

- The specific five-command set (menu bar timer, start/stop hotkey, live timer view, entries list, open dashboard) and their titles, subtitles and keywords
- The running timer as the authoritative session-scoped state, and the `{ runningId, at }` shape of its echo record
- The five entry fields and the single composition component shared by the start, log-past and edit forms
- Adopting a project's billable default in composers but not in the editor, because a rate may already be snapshotted
- The description autocomplete pushed list, and the deliberate split between recents (keyed on the whole description/project/task/billable combination) and suggestions (keyed on the case-folded description alone)
- The catalog forms (client/project/task/tag), the shared colour palette, and creating a catalog row mid-timer
- Idle vs running menu bar title modes, and the rule that an idle total is `36m` and never `0:36`
- The recency windows (7-day resume, 14-day list, 180-day descriptions) and the requirement that manifest and README claims match them
- The stop-with-an-id idempotency rule and the no-id 404, and the `isAlreadyStopped` client reading
- Not rolling a backwards end forward past midnight in the launcher because both pickers carry their own date
- The absence of invoice tools because the public API has no invoice routes, and the money-visibility refusal hint
- The concrete tool list (get_running_timer, list_entries, start_timer, stop_timer, log_time_entry, list_clients/projects/tasks/tags, create_*, summary_report, get_token_info) and the concrete capability names (entries:read, entries:write, catalog:read, catalog:write, reports:read)
- The MCP instructions paragraph's content (entries carry an optional project which may belong to a client, durations in seconds, invoices stay in the web app)
- Project/task/client name enrichment on timer results
- Reports reading a bare `to` as the end of the day in the server's zone while the entry list reads it as midnight UTC — the ROUTE-SPECIFIC disagreement is domain; the invariant (resolve ambiguous boundaries in the tool) is generic
- The queued-work descriptions shared with the devices settings screen, and the hold reasons enumerated for held rows
