<a id="desktop"></a>

## Desktop (Electron)

**Key:** `desktop` · **Starter today:** partial · **Open rows:** 29

The reference ships a complete, hardened Electron wrapper: a privileged custom scheme serving a dedicated static export, a trust-checked IPC surface, an OS-secret-store credential path that refuses insecure backends, a headless mode built for agents and CI, a six-channel signing/packaging matrix that fails closed, electron-updater restricted to direct downloads with a text-edited staged rollout, a draft-release job that verifies every feed entry, and a Playwright harness that drives the same main bundle over the app origin while proving auth from the server side (`electron/src/`, `electron-builder.config.mjs`, `scripts/build-desktop.mjs`, `scripts/lib/desktop-release.mjs`, `.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml`, `e2e/desktop/`). The starter has a 250-line demo shell: one `BrowserWindow`, three untyped `ipcMain.handle` calls with no sender check, `loadFile()` over `file://`, an inline `build` block with no signing, fuses, notarization or updater, and a release workflow that uploads unsigned artifacts. Two starter defects are load-bearing: `cli/src/scaffold/native-origins.ts:40` already trusts `app://-` for the desktop feature and `starter/CLAUDE.md:193-196` tells the user `file://` is untrustworthy, but `starter/electron/main.ts:180` still loads `file://`; and `build:desktop` never sets `NEXT_FILE_EXPORT=1`, so `packages/client/out/` — the directory the electron-builder `files` glob and `loadFile` both name — is never produced. The starter also sets `assetPrefix: "./"` for exports, which is exactly what makes a rooted custom scheme 404 every chunk on every route but `/`.

### Gaps

- [ ] **Serve the packaged bundle from a privileged custom scheme, never file://**
  - Register a custom scheme as standard + secure + fetch-capable + CORS-enabled + streaming BEFORE the app-ready event, then answer requests on it from the export directory with a content type, nosniff and a CSP header. The packaged window loads <scheme>://<host>/ instead of a file path. The starter loads packages/client/out/index.html through loadFile (starter/electron/main.ts:180-183) while cli/src/scaffold/native-origins.ts:40 already adds app://- to the backend's trusted origins and starter/CLAUDE.md:193-196 tells the user to register the scheme themselves — the scaffold promises an origin the shell does not serve.
  - *Invariant:* The scheme and host together ARE the document origin. They key per-origin browser storage and must appear verbatim in the backend's trusted-origin allowlist; a file:// document serialises its origin to "null", which no allowlist can match, so credentialed sign-in answers 403 and per-origin storage is not persisted. Once a version has shipped, changing either the scheme or the host silently strands every installed copy's local storage and breaks its server trust entry.
  - *Reference:* `electron/src/protocol.ts:1-38`
- [ ] **Resolve scheme paths through a pure, traversal-proof function that mirrors the web server's rules**
  - Extract the URL-to-file mapping into a dependency-injected pure function (filesystem probe passed in) with its own unit tests: directory to index.html, bare path to <path>.html, everything else to the 404 document with a 404 status, host mismatch to 404, malformed percent-escape to 400. Reject traversal before any filesystem call.
  - *Invariant:* Split path segments on BOTH separators on every OS — a decoded %5C is a separator to the Windows path module — and reject "..", NUL and any resolved path that is not the root or inside it. The fallback chain must match whatever the web server does for the same export, or routes that work on the web 404 only in the packaged app, where nobody is testing them.
  - *Reference:* `electron/src/resolve-app-path.ts:40-89`
- [ ] **Give the desktop target its own export directory and verify the baked backend origin**
  - One build script owns an export directory used by no other target or test run. It requires the backend-origin env var, rejects a URL with a path/query/fragment, greps the emitted JS chunks for the literal value, walks every emitted HTML file and refuses a relative asset prefix, refuses to build while a dev server owns the framework's cache directory, and writes a build-target marker so a later packaging run can say what the export on disk was built against.
  - *Invariant:* The backend origin is inlined at build time. Unset, every request resolves against the app's own origin and the build stays green. And a relative asset prefix 404s every chunk on every route but "/" once the app is served from a scheme with a root — so the export used by a rooted custom scheme must be root-absolute. The starter currently sets assetPrefix "./" for exports (starter/packages/client/next.config.ts:34) while aiming at a rooted app:// origin; the two cannot both be right.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/build-desktop.mjs:163-259`
- [ ] **Wire the export mode into the desktop build command**
  - The desktop build command must set whatever env flag switches the client to static-export output, and the packager's file globs plus the shell's load path must name the directory that flag actually writes.
  - *Invariant:* The packaged file list, the shell's load path and the build command must agree on one export directory, or the package is built from an empty glob and the shell loads nothing — with every step exiting zero. In the starter, build:desktop is build:client + electron:compile (starter/package.json:30) and build:client never sets NEXT_FILE_EXPORT=1, so output:"export" never activates and packages/client/out/ — named by both the files glob (starter/package.json:85) and loadFile — is never produced.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/build-desktop.mjs:216-259`
- [ ] **Route every IPC handler through a sender-trust wrapper**
  - A single handle() helper registers all main-process IPC and rejects any call whose sender frame URL is not the app's own document (or, unpackaged only, the configured dev URL). Handlers throw a named error the renderer can distinguish. The dev URL is read only when the app is not packaged.
  - *Invariant:* The preload is attached per WINDOW, not per URL: a window that ever navigated somewhere else — a mistaken navigation, a data: page, a detached frame — still holds a working bridge unless every handler re-checks the sender. And a packaged build must ignore any dev-URL env var, or anything able to set an environment variable can load an arbitrary page with the full bridge attached. The starter registers three bare ipcMain.handle calls with no sender check at all (starter/electron/main.ts:16,52,68,76).
  - *Reference:* `electron/src/ipc.ts:32-44`
- [ ] **Apply the security baseline to every webContents, not to the one window you know about**
  - Subscribe to web-contents-created and install, on each: a window-open handler that always denies and hands http(s)/mailto to the OS, a will-navigate guard that denies anything off-origin and hands it over instead, a will-redirect guard, and a will-attach-webview refusal. On the default session install BOTH the permission-request handler and the permission-check handler, defaulting to deny with a small named allowlist scoped to the app's own documents.
  - *Invariant:* The request handler alone is not enough — the synchronous permission-check path is separate, and a page can pass one while failing the other. A blanket deny breaks clipboard writes (the sanitized-write permission gates the clipboard API), so the allowlist must be explicit rather than empty. Only http(s) and mailto may be handed to the OS: an arbitrary custom scheme handed to the shell launches whatever handler is registered for it. The starter guards only the one window it constructs, allows same-origin window.open, and installs no permission handler at all (starter/electron/main.ts:113-144).
  - *Reference:* `electron/src/security.ts:32-67`
- [ ] **Deliver the CSP as a response header from the scheme handler**
  - Define the policy in one module and attach it as a content-security-policy response header on HTML responses served by the custom scheme, rather than as a meta tag inside the exported HTML.
  - *Invariant:* The export is shared with the web build, so a meta CSP in it changes the web app too and the two can no longer be compared byte for byte. The script-src must allow inline script if the framework inlines its bootstrap/payload; leaving remote script hosts out silently disables any third-party snippet inside the shell, which is a decision to make on purpose rather than discover. The starter has no CSP anywhere in the shell.
  - *Reference:* `electron/src/csp.ts:1-30`
- [ ] **Harden webPreferences, the production menu, and the build-time fuses**
  - Window: sandbox on, context isolation on, node integration off, web security on, devTools only in dev, background throttling off for a window that spends the day hidden. Menu: no reload/force-reload/toggle-devtools items or accelerators outside dev. Package: flip the fuses that disable run-as-node, the NODE_OPTIONS env var and inspect arguments, and enable asar integrity, load-only-from-asar and cookie encryption.
  - *Invariant:* Fuses rewrite the binary, so they must be flipped BEFORE signing or the signature breaks; an ad-hoc-signed arm64 build must then be re-signed, or the OS refuses to launch it. The inspector fuse also means the packaged build can no longer be driven by the standard Electron test launcher — the E2E harness must drive the unpackaged main bundle instead (or attach over a remote debugging port), and discovering this only when the release build times out in CI is the expensive path. The starter sets contextIsolation and nodeIntegration but not sandbox, leaves devTools and the reload/devtools menu roles enabled in production (starter/electron/main.ts:96-100,216-225), and configures no fuses.
  - *Reference:* `electron-builder.config.mjs:70-79`
- [ ] **Pin the profile directory by name and separate packaged, unpackaged and test profiles**
  - Compute the user-data directory from explicit constants — one name packaged, another unpackaged, a suffix for headless — with an env-var override, and set it before anything else touches the app's paths.
  - *Invariant:* The framework derives the default profile path from the packaged manifest's display name, falling back to its package name. Adding or changing a display name later silently moves every installed copy to an empty profile and strands whatever was queued in the old one. The profile also carries the single-instance lock, so it must be set before the lock is requested, or an unpackaged dev run exits instantly and without a word because the installed app holds the lock. And a test profile must never be the installed app's: a test run using a mock secret backend cannot decrypt the real profile's credential file, and a store that deletes what it cannot decrypt would sign the real user out.
  - *Reference:* `electron/src/profile.ts:1-40`
- [ ] **Single-instance lock, hide-on-close and persisted window state**
  - Request the single-instance lock and exit the second launch after revealing the first. Persist bounds, maximised and fullscreen state to a file in the profile, debounced on resize/move and written synchronously on close. Restore maximised state only after ready-to-show. Close hides rather than quits on macOS always, and elsewhere while a tray icon exists to bring the window back.
  - *Invariant:* Two instances on one profile both write the same origin-keyed local storage. maximize() also SHOWS a hidden window, so calling it right after construction defeats the show:false anti-flash and makes a headless launch visible whenever the last session was maximised. A quit-for-update must set a quitting flag first, because the OS updater closes windows before the before-quit event and the hide-on-close handler would otherwise cancel the restart. The starter has no lock, no hide-on-close, and persists only a fullscreen boolean (starter/electron/main.ts:27-50).
  - *Reference:* `electron/src/window.ts:19-32,112-161`
- [ ] **Keep the session credential in the main process, in the OS secret store, and refuse insecure backends**
  - A main-process-only store: read/write a ciphertext file in the profile (owner-only mode, temp-file + rename), encrypted through the platform secret API, reachable from the renderer only via named bridge calls that never expose the path or the key. Expose a status the UI can show. Factor the logic over injected encryption and file interfaces so every branch is unit-tested without the runtime.
  - *Invariant:* On a machine with no keyring the platform secret API still reports success, using a key hardcoded into the browser engine — that is obfuscation, not encryption. Persisting a credential under it looks secure and is not, so the store must refuse to write, keep the value in memory for that run, start the next launch signed out, and SAY SO in the UI. Ciphertext that no longer decrypts (new keychain, profile copied to another machine) must be deleted rather than retried forever. And the HTTP client must send credentials:"omit" inside the shell, so a second, silent cookie credential cannot mask a broken token path. The starter has no secret storage; its only persisted state is a plaintext prefs.json.
  - *Reference:* `electron/src/secure-store.ts:1-143`
- [ ] **Define the preload bridge as one typed contract in the shared package**
  - Put the bridge interface and the IPC channel-name constants in the workspace's shared package. The preload builds an object typed as that interface; the renderer declares the same type on Window by importing it. Wrap every event listener so the raw IPC event never crosses the context bridge, and return an unsubscribe function from each.
  - *Invariant:* The contract file may contain types and string constants only — no Node, no runtime API, no DOM — because it is bundled into a sandboxed preload AND imported by the renderer. If the two sides declare the shape separately they drift without a type error; the starter does exactly that (starter/electron/preload.ts:12 exposes an untyped object literal, starter/packages/client/src/types/electron.d.ts:9 re-declares the shape by hand). Passing the IPC event straight to a renderer listener leaks its sender across the bridge.
  - *Reference:* `packages/shared/src/desktop-bridge.ts:1-13`
- [ ] **Bundle main and preload, pack nothing else, and verify the archive after packaging**
  - Bundle both entry points to CommonJS with only the runtime marked external, emit into a dist directory, copy any needed static assets beside them. The packager's file list contains only that dist, the export and the manifest — plus an explicit negation of the dependency directory. After packaging, list every archive this run wrote and assert it contains nothing outside the allowed prefixes, that no unrelated workspace dependency leaked in, and that the packed version equals the source version (and, on a tag, the tag).
  - *Invariant:* The packager adds the app's production dependencies to the archive regardless of what the file list says — only an explicit negation keeps them out, which matters when the repo root's dependencies belong to a different native target. A release whose archive reports a different version than the release name is never offered its own update, and nothing else will tell you. The preload runs sandboxed, where require resolves only the runtime's renderer modules, so everything else must be inlined at build time. The starter compiles with tsc (no bundling) and lists three files with no dependency negation (starter/package.json:31,84-89).
  - *Reference:* `scripts/build-desktop.mjs:329-350`
- [ ] **Build a headless mode for tests and agents into the shell itself**
  - An env flag that makes the app run, load and answer IPC while never showing, focusing or activating a window; on macOS set the accessory activation policy before ready and hide the dock icon. Append the engine's mock-keychain switch. Record external-URL opens on a global and stdout instead of performing them. Give headless runs their own profile unless one is named explicitly. Create no tray, register no OS shortcut, post no notification and touch no login item — record each on a test-hook global instead.
  - *Invariant:* Without this, every test run steals keyboard focus and flashes a window across the screen of whoever is using the machine, and the OS may raise a system password prompt when the real keychain sees a rebuilt binary whose signature differs from the item's creator. A hidden window keeps painting, but reading a frame back out of one is per-platform: on macOS it keeps a compositor surface and screenshots work; on X11 an unmapped window has no surface, the capture is queued and never answered, and the caller hangs until it times out — so the render proof on that platform must be a separate, window-shown smoke test. The starter has no headless path at all.
  - *Reference:* `electron/src/headless.ts:1-22`
- [ ] **Add a desktop E2E harness that drives the real main bundle over the app origin**
  - A separate test project with its own global setup that starts the app's own backend and rebuilds the export when the one on disk targets a different origin. Each spec launches the same unpackaged main bundle with a fresh profile directory, forced headless, and with the dev-URL and run-as-node env vars deleted from the inherited environment. Collect page errors and CSP-violation console messages per navigation. Preload a request recorder into the backend that logs each request's origin, client label, auth scheme and whether a cookie was present, and mark the harness's own out-of-band calls by user-agent so they can be excluded.
  - *Invariant:* The packaged build cannot be driven by the standard launcher once the inspector fuse is set, so the harness must run the unpackaged main bundle — the same scheme, export and main process, minus the fuses. An inherited run-as-node variable turns the binary into plain Node and the launch hangs; an inherited dev URL silently tests the wrong document. The export is reused between runs, so a renderer change is NOT in it unless a rebuild flag is passed. Asserting auth from the client side proves nothing: only the server-side log shows that the shell sent a bearer token and no cookie. The starter's e2e suite is browser-only; nothing launches the shell.
  - *Reference:* `e2e/desktop/support.ts:33-80,218-244`
- [ ] **Detect the distribution channel at runtime**
  - A pure function mapping (platform, packaged, store-build flags, package-manager env vars) to a named channel, with each channel a unit test rather than a build for another OS. Two behaviours derive from it: whether the app may replace itself, and how open-at-login works.
  - *Invariant:* One binary is built per target, but the runtime still has to know which channel installed it, because a store or package manager owns the files: an in-app updater there either cannot write to its own bundle (sandbox, immutable package format, read-only mount) or fights the manager — and it fails quietly, looking like an update that never arrives. The same is true of open-at-login: two packaging formats have no autostart path reachable from the runtime and must report that rather than silently doing nothing.
  - *Reference:* `electron/src/distribution.ts:47-111`
- [ ] **Restrict auto-update to direct downloads and make the absent feed explicit**
  - A pure policy function returning enabled, or disabled with a named reason (unpackaged, store, sandbox, package-manager, no-feed, turned-off by env). The updater module is required lazily inside the load callback so a disabled build never executes its module code. A separate status reducer covers every updater event, and a headless run swaps in an in-memory updater driven only by the test hook.
  - *Invariant:* Leave the packager's publish key undefined and it guesses a repository feed from a token that every CI runner has in its environment — so it must be set to an EXPLICIT null when there is no feed. Signed platforms only get a feed when signed: the macOS update framework refuses to install into an unsigned app, and an unsigned test build must never be able to replace itself with a release. The presence of the generated feed file beside the app is what the runtime checks, not a hardcoded assumption.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/lib/desktop-release.mjs:252-256`
- [ ] **Never restart for an update on the person's behalf**
  - Set install-on-quit, and give quit-and-install exactly one call site reached only from an explicit user action (a tray item and a settings button). Keep a test that greps the source to hold that invariant. Call the quitting flag first.
  - *Invariant:* A restart the person did not ask for loses whatever they were typing; a queued offline write survives it, a half-filled form does not. The single-call-site rule only holds if something mechanically enforces it — a second caller added later fails silently, in the field, months after the review that would have caught it.
  - *Reference:* `electron/src/updater-model.ts:20-24`
- [ ] **Stage the rollout by rewriting one line of each feed as text**
  - Two entry points — CI on a tag and a person after publishing — both go through one rewrite function: parse the feed, edit only the column-zero staging key as text, re-parse, and assert the result equals the input with nothing but that key changed. A percentage parser accepts a whole number 0-100 with an optional percent sign and throws on anything else; validate it in a seconds-long job before any build, and pass it through the environment rather than interpolating a free-text input into a script.
  - *Invariant:* The feed must never be re-serialised: each file's hash and size must stay byte-for-byte as the packager wrote them, because the updater verifies them and a mismatch errors on every installed copy. An explicit 100 is NOT everybody — the membership comparison is strictly-less-than, so one identity in 2^32 is excluded; a full rollout therefore REMOVES the key. The membership identity is a random id stored in the profile directory, stable only because that directory is pinned by name, so the membership predicate must never be overridden in app code. Lowering a percentage takes nobody back; nothing rolls an update back, you fix forward.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/lib/desktop-rollout.mjs:1-124`
- [ ] **Make signing fail closed, per channel, with explicit trigger variables**
  - Declare each channel's required credential sets as data. No credential set at all builds artifacts whose FILE NAMES say unsigned; one complete set signs; a partial set throws before anything is exported. Name a subset of each set as the triggers that mean "this channel is meant to be signed". Two complete sets for one channel refuse. Every mode turns the packager's keychain identity auto-discovery off, and the unsigned and store modes delete the signing variables from the child environment.
  - *Invariant:* A half-configured credential set is a typo that would otherwise ship an unsigned build under a signed build's name. Some credentials are shared with other release pipelines in the same repo, so counting every variable would make a repo that ships a different native target look like it is asking for a signed desktop build — hence triggers. Without identity auto-discovery off, the packager finds a local certificate in the developer's keychain and signs even a directory-only build, which is slow, differs per machine, and makes an artifact named "unsigned" a lie. The starter's workflow passes nothing but a CI token and uploads whatever falls out (starter/.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml:55-64).
  - *Reference:* `scripts/lib/desktop-release.mjs:110-178`
- [ ] **Gate the store and platform-specific packaging so a misconfiguration cannot ship**
  - Hardened runtime and notarization only with a real identity. The store target sets identity to null when unsigned rather than falling back to ad-hoc. Sandbox entitlements are generated per build because they name the team. The store-package identity fields are read from the environment and never defaulted, and the store channel may build its target and no other. After packaging, assert the platform manifest keys that only one config block feeds actually arrived.
  - *Invariant:* An ad-hoc signature has no team id, so the hardened runtime's library validation refuses the runtime framework at launch and the dynamic loader aborts before any app code runs — the app simply does not open, with no useful message. A store package whose identity does not match the reserved product name is refused on upload, so inventing a default only hides the problem until submission. The packager builds the platform manifest from one config block and silently drops the store-specific one, so the keys the store requires must be placed in the block it actually reads and then verified on disk. And the store package target built outside its own channel gets placeholder identity values and looks like a valid upload.
  - *Reference:* `electron-builder.config.mjs:85-137`
- [ ] **A tag builds a DRAFT release; a person publishes it**
  - On a tag, a job collects the matrix legs, attaches signed downloads and each platform's update feed to a DRAFT release, replaces per-leg checksum files with one over what is attached, and never touches an already-published release. Skip unsigned files and store-only package formats. Warn per platform that produced no download. Then verify every file each feed names is attached with exactly the hash and size the feed states, and fail if a leg that should carry a feed did not bring one.
  - *Invariant:* The updater reads only PUBLISHED releases, so publishing the draft IS the release decision — and a package-manager formula that installs the same build must declare that the app updates itself, or the two fight. If a feed names a file that is missing or whose hash differs, every installed copy downloads it, rejects the checksum and reports an error; if a leg silently ships without its feed file, installed copies are never offered the release and nothing anywhere says so.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/lib/desktop-release.mjs:412-467`
- [ ] **Read build-time public config from a repo variable with a literal fallback, never a secret**
  - The release workflow reads the backend origin (and any other value inlined into the client bundle) from a repository VARIABLE, falling back to a literal default, and prints which it used.
  - *Invariant:* An unset secret expands to the empty string. The build then succeeds and ships a binary that talks to no backend — the single most expensive way to discover a missing configuration. A public origin is not a secret and must not be stored as one. The starter's workflow reads it from secrets (starter/.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml:50) with no fallback and no assertion that it landed in the bundle.
  - *Reference:* `.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml:50-59`
- [ ] **Guarantee the runtime binary is installed before anything tries to launch it**
  - A small script that inspects the runtime package for its path marker, its version file and the executable itself, runs the package's own installer when any is missing, and re-inspects afterwards. Every consumer — the build, the E2E harness, CI — calls it first. Failure messages name the supported toolchain version and the files to delete.
  - *Invariant:* Recent versions of the runtime package ship NO install script: the binary is fetched the first time something requires it, so a fresh dependency install leaves it absent and the first consumer downloads a hundred-plus megabytes mid-run or fails outright. Allow-listing the package's build scripts does nothing, because there is no build to allow. Worse, under some toolchain majors the extraction stops partway and still exits zero — so the check must be for the marker files and the version file, never the exit code.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/ensure-electron.mjs:1-71`
- [ ] **Verify the other desktop platforms from one development machine**
  - Two project-agnostic tools: a drop-folder publisher that copies an unpacked build plus a double-click launcher into one shared folder that VMs mount, and a containerised smoke test that starts the unpacked build under a virtual X server, drives it over the debugging protocol and saves a screenshot with a pass/fail result. Project wiring stays in the project's own scripts; the tools import nothing from the repo around them and name no app.
  - *Invariant:* The smoke test must run with the window SHOWN, not headless: on X11 a never-shown window gives the debugging protocol no frames and the screenshot hangs until the caller times out. That makes the visible-window smoke test the only proof the app renders on that platform, which is exactly why it cannot be made headless "for consistency". Keeping the tools free of project imports is what lets them be shared at all.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/crossplat/README.md:1-16`
- [ ] **Derive package-manager manifests from one artifact-name module**
  - Templates for each package manager live in a packaging directory with placeholder checksums, rendered from a published release by a script. The artifact name patterns are defined once and consumed by both the packager config and the manifest renderer. The renderer refuses any placeholder with a missing or empty value.
  - *Invariant:* If the packager config and the manifests spell artifact names separately, a rename leaves a manifest pointing at a file no release contains — and a package manager only tells you after submission. A manifest written with a blank checksum is likewise rejected downstream, long after the release looked finished. Manifests are only ever rendered from SIGNED artifacts, so an unsigned build can never satisfy one.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/lib/desktop-release.mjs:264-285,341-377`
- [ ] **Implement open-at-login per distribution channel, and say so when it is impossible**
  - A pure function mapping a channel to a mechanism: the runtime's own login-item API, an XDG autostart entry naming a specific executable path, or an explicit unsupported reason. A launch started by the OS at login passes a marker argument and opens to the tray without a window, where a tray exists. Headless and unpackaged runs get an in-memory backend.
  - *Invariant:* Two packaging formats have no reachable autostart path at all — a virtualised registry key that is never read at login, and a sandbox whose autostart request the runtime does not expose — so the UI must report that instead of showing a toggle that does nothing. For the self-contained-image format, the executable path lives inside a mount that disappears on exit, so the autostart entry must name the image's own environment variable. And a test run must never register the development runtime's binary as a login item on someone's machine: it would outlive the test.
  - *Reference:* `electron/src/distribution.ts:69-111`
- [ ] **Mark the shell on the document before first paint and style only window chrome**
  - A pre-paint inline script sets a shell class and a platform attribute on the document element; a stylesheet keyed off them defines the drag region on the app header and, on macOS, an inset so the window controls of a title-bar-less window clear the app's own chrome. The window is created with a background colour matching the theme and is not shown until the first frame is ready.
  - *Invariant:* The marker must be set before the first paint, or the window chrome jumps once the app hydrates. It must be a DIFFERENT marker from the mobile shell's — reusing one means a desktop window styled as a phone. A default white window background is a white flash on every dark-mode launch, which show:false alone does not fix. (Read from the reference's rules; I did not open the pre-paint or stylesheet source.)
  - *Reference:* `CLAUDE.md:227-232`
- [ ] **Never let the generator overwrite a customised shell directory**
  - Adding or updating the desktop feature on an existing project must treat the shell directory as user-owned: copy it only when absent, and otherwise report what would change rather than writing it.
  - *Invariant:* The shell is the file a project customises first and most — window chrome, tray, IPC surface, protocol handler. A scaffolding tool that re-copies it on update silently discards that work, and there is no diff in the generated project to notice it by. The reference records this as an explicit answered question against the generator.
  - *Reference:* `docs/desktop-app-plan.md:23-24`

### Identifiers this area freezes

Every name below becomes a contract the first time this area ships. All of
them must come from the manifest's `identifiers` block — see
`cli/src/scaffold/identifiers.ts`. An area that spells one of these out as a
literal has re-introduced the divergence that block exists to prevent.

- Desktop document origin as scheme + host (reference: app://-). It keys per-origin browser storage and must appear verbatim in the backend's trusted-origin allowlist; already modelled as ids.desktopOrigin in cli/src/scaffold/identifiers.ts:133,276,378-384 and as ORIGINS_BY_FEATURE.desktop in cli/src/scaffold/native-origins.ts:40.
- Reverse-DNS application id (electron-builder appId, the Windows AppUserModelId set in main, the macOS application-group prefix, the store product identity). Already ids.bundleId, checked across files by cli/src/scaffold/identifier-agreement.ts:110-118.
- Product / display name: the packager's productName also determines the default profile directory, which is why the profile is pinned by an independent constant instead.
- Profile directory names: the packaged name, the unpackaged name and the headless suffix (reference: trackyourtime, trackyourtime-dev, -headless). Changing them strands installed profiles.
- Credential ciphertext file name inside the profile (reference: session.bin) and the window-state file name (window-state.json).
- The rollout identity file location — it lives under the profile directory, so pinning the profile name is what makes rollout membership stable across updates.
- Environment variable names the shell reads: the headless flag, the user-data-dir override, the dev-URL variable and the disable-updates kill switch (reference: TRACKYOURTIME_HEADLESS, TRACKYOURTIME_USER_DATA_DIR, ELECTRON_DEV_URL, TRACKYOURTIME_DISABLE_UPDATES). Tests, CI and managed-machine policies all hardcode them.
- Test-hook global names on globalThis (reference: __trackYourTimeDesktop, __trackYourTimeOpenedExternally). Specs reference them by literal string.
- IPC channel name strings in the shared bridge contract — the preload and main must agree, and a rename is invisible to the type system if either side spells one inline.
- Linux executable name and the XDG autostart .desktop file name (reference: trackyourtime, trackyourtime.desktop).
- Store package identity: the AppX applicationId and the snap command name, both of which the autostart path and the store listing depend on.
- Artifact file-name patterns (reference: TrackYourTime-${version}-mac-${arch}.${ext} and siblings). Package-manager manifests point at these names, so they cannot change after a manifest is submitted.
- The update feed's repository coordinates (owner/repo) baked into the packager config and therefore into every shipped app-update.yml.
- Flatpak application id / metainfo id in the packaging templates (reference: com.ricoslabs.trackyourtime).

### Domain-specific — do not extract

- The tray menu and global-shortcut surface as built: it publishes a running timer, recent entries, an unsent count and translated labels from the renderer, and takes commands back into entry mutations (electron/src/desktop.ts, tray-model.ts). The generic residue worth keeping is only the shape — a renderer-published state snapshot rendered by main-process OS affordances, with headless runs recording instead of creating them — not the timer payload.
- decideTimerToggle, the toggle rule shared between the desktop shortcut and the Raycast extension (CLAUDE.md:270-272), and the single default accelerator chosen for it.
- The entire desktop activity-capture subsystem (electron/src/activity/, ~25 files): frontmost-application polling per OS, a per-day JSONL segment store, time-span suggestions, accept/dismiss holds. It is a product feature of a time tracker, not desktop-shell scaffolding. Its channel-gating pattern (activityCaptureSupport in distribution.ts) is already captured generically as 'the runtime must know its channel because capabilities differ per channel'.
- The offline queue in localStorage and its replay semantics, which is where the 'never change the origin' invariant bites hardest for this product. The invariant is kept generically as 'the origin keys per-origin storage'; the queue itself is domain.
- The /download page's per-channel link table and its null-until-published rule (lib/site-links.ts), which is marketing-site surface for one product.
- The two-factor and device-code E2E helpers in e2e/desktop/support.ts:256-294 and the 'Sign in with your browser' device flow with client id trackyourtime-desktop — these belong to the auth area, not the desktop shell.
- The concrete identifiers themselves: com.ricoslabs.trackyourtime, 'Track Your Time', trackyourtime.dev, api.trackyourtime.dev, the trebeljahr/trackyourtime feed, and the brand tray icon set — all scaffold-time values, listed under frozenIdentifiers rather than copied.
- prod:desktop / prod:win as written (a person checking real apps against live production servers, explicitly never run by agents or tests) — the mechanism generalises but the 'point it at production' policy is a project decision, not a template default.

### Reference evidence

- Privileged standard scheme registered before app-ready (standard, secure, supportFetchAPI, corsEnabled, stream) and a protocol.handle that serves the export with nosniff + CSP on HTML — `electron/src/protocol.ts:22-72`
- Pure, unit-tested path resolver mirroring the web server's trailingSlash rules, refusing .., encoded separators, encoded dots and NUL before touching the filesystem — `electron/src/resolve-app-path.ts:40-117`
- IPC wrapper that refuses any sender frame outside the app origin (or the dev URL, unpackaged only) — `electron/src/ipc.ts:32-44`
- Trust predicates: app-origin check, dev-URL check that rejects opaque-origin schemes, OS-handoff allowlist (http/https/mailto), permission allowlist — `electron/src/trust.ts:9-88`
- Security baseline applied to every webContents ever created: window-open handler, will-navigate, will-redirect, will-attach-webview, permission request AND check handlers — `electron/src/security.ts:32-67`
- CSP delivered as a response header from the scheme handler, so the shared web export's HTML stays byte-identical — `electron/src/csp.ts:16-30`
- webPreferences hardening: sandbox, contextIsolation, nodeIntegration off, devTools only in dev, backgroundThrottling off for a long-hidden window; maximize deferred to ready-to-show — `electron/src/window.ts:94-121`
- markQuitting() so an update restart is not swallowed by the hide-on-close handler (the OS updater closes windows before before-quit) — `electron/src/window.ts:24-32`
- electronFuses: runAsNode/NODE_OPTIONS/inspect off, asar integrity + onlyLoadAppFromAsar, cookie encryption, resetAdHocDarwinSignature — `electron-builder.config.mjs:70-79`
- Production menu with no Reload/ForceReload/ToggleDevTools and no accelerators for them — `electron/src/menu.ts:1-28`
- Profile directory pinned by name, separate for packaged/unpackaged/headless, overridable by env; set before the single-instance lock is requested — `electron/src/profile.ts:27-40`
- Single-instance lock, accessory activation policy and mock-keychain switch before ready, app-scheme registration, security and IPC trust installed at start — `electron/src/main.ts:40-101`
- Secret store over the OS keychain that refuses to persist on basic_text/unknown backends, keeps the credential in memory for the run, deletes ciphertext it cannot decrypt, writes 0600 atomically — `electron/src/secure-store.ts:31-136`
- Bridge contract in the shared package, types and string constants only, imported by both the preload and the renderer's Window declaration — `packages/shared/src/desktop-bridge.ts:1-40`
- Preload exposes only the contract object and wraps every listener so the IPC event (and its sender) never crosses the context bridge — `electron/src/preload.ts:23-113`
- Headless mode: env flag, never shown/focused, no dock icon, documented per-platform frame-capture behaviour — `electron/src/headless.ts:1-22`
- External URL handoff recorded on a global and stdout instead of performed, in headless — `electron/src/external.ts:20-30`
- Build script: own export dir, required API origin env, chunk grep for the literal, refusal of "./_next" in any emitted HTML, dev-server lock check, build-target marker — `scripts/build-desktop.mjs:163-259`
- esbuild bundles main and preload to CJS with only electron external; tray assets copied beside the bundle — `scripts/build-desktop.mjs:263-290`
- Post-package verification: asar listing must contain only the bundle, export and package.json; packed version must equal source version; macOS Info.plist keys asserted per store target — `scripts/build-desktop.mjs:329-378`
- files list with explicit !node_modules/** because electron-builder adds production dependencies regardless — `electron-builder.config.mjs:47-56`
- hardenedRuntime and notarize gated on a real identity; store target uses identity:null when unsigned; generated per-build sandbox entitlements; ITSAppUsesNonExemptEncryption and team id in the one plist block the packager reads — `electron-builder.config.mjs:85-137`
- Signing resolution: complete set signs, empty set names the artifact -unsigned, partial set throws; trigger variables so credentials shared with another pipeline do not imply intent; two complete sets refuse — `scripts/lib/desktop-release.mjs:110-150`
- builderEnvFor turns identity auto-discovery off for every mode and strips signing credentials from unsigned and store builds — `scripts/lib/desktop-release.mjs:161-178`
- targetChannelProblem: the store package target may only be built by its own channel, or the packager fills the identity with placeholders — `scripts/lib/desktop-release.mjs:208-221`
- updateFeedFor returns an explicit null rather than undefined, because undefined makes electron-builder guess a repository feed from the CI token — `scripts/lib/desktop-release.mjs:252-256`
- Artifact name patterns in one module shared by the packager config and the manifest renderer; manifest renderer refuses blank values — `scripts/lib/desktop-release.mjs:264-285,341-377`
- tagMismatch: on a tag run the tag must name the packaged version — `scripts/lib/desktop-release.mjs:292-295`
- releasePlan and feedProblems: never attach unsigned or store-only files, require each leg's feed file, verify every file a feed names is attached with the stated sha512 and size — `scripts/lib/desktop-release.mjs:412-467`
- Distribution channel detection from process flags and package-manager env vars; selfUpdates and loginItemMechanism derived from it — `electron/src/distribution.ts:47-111`
- Updater policy: unpackaged, store, sandbox, package-manager and no-feed all disable; a kill-switch env var — `electron/src/updater-model.ts:43-68`
- electron-updater required lazily inside loadUpdater so a store or package build never runs its module code; memory updater in headless — `electron/src/updater.ts:48-104`
- Staged rollout rewritten as one top-level text line, proved by parsing both versions and diffing everything but that key; 100 removes the key; never override isUserWithinRollout — `scripts/lib/desktop-rollout.mjs:29-124`
- Rollout identity is <userData>/.updaterId, stable only because userData is pinned by name — `electron/src/updater.ts:16-26`
- Desktop e2e harness: strips inherited dev-URL and run-as-node env, forces headless, fresh user-data dir, launches the same unpackaged main bundle — `e2e/desktop/support.ts:33-80`
- Server-side request log read by the specs (origin, client, auth scheme, cookie presence), with the harness's own Node calls excluded by user-agent — `e2e/desktop/support.ts:218-244`
- Separate Playwright project with its own global setup that builds the export when the one on disk targets another API origin — `e2e/desktop/playwright.config.ts:3-28`
- Runtime-binary guard: the package has no install script, so the binary is downloaded explicitly and a truncated extraction is detected by marker files rather than the exit code — `scripts/ensure-electron.mjs:31-71`
- Six-leg release matrix, one per distribution channel, with per-channel artifact globs — `.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml:86-140`
- Build-time API origin read from a repo VARIABLE with a literal fallback, never a secret, because an unset secret expands to empty — `.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml:50-59`
- Rollout percentage validated in a seconds-long job before any build, passed through the environment rather than interpolated — `.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml:66-84`
- Login item per channel: Electron API, XDG autostart file naming the image variable, or an explicit unsupported reason; memory backend for headless and unpackaged — `electron/src/login-item.ts:1-40`
- Package-manager manifest templates filled from a published release — `packaging/`
- Project-agnostic cross-platform tooling (VM drop folder, Linux smoke under Xvfb in Docker) that imports nothing from the repo and is explicitly earmarked for hatchkit — `scripts/crossplat/README.md:1-16`
- The Linux smoke test runs with the window SHOWN because on X11 a never-shown window gives CDP no frames and the capture hangs — `CLAUDE.md:346-359`
- Shell marker class and platform attribute set pre-paint, styling window chrome only — `CLAUDE.md:227-232`
- Plan doc answer 7: the generator must never rewrite a customised desktop directory — `docs/desktop-app-plan.md:23-24`

<a id="mobile"></a>

## Mobile (Capacitor)

**Key:** `mobile` · **Starter today:** partial · **Open rows:** 41

The reference ships a fully productionised Capacitor surface: both native trees committed, a single `build:mobile` script that preflights the toolchain and asserts the export before syncing, three-tier on-device storage (host secret store / durable app KV / web storage), a radio-backed network verdict, a resume-order contract, safe-area CSS that is inert on web by construction, and a plan-gated release workflow that refuses to produce release-looking artifacts without a complete secret set. The Hatchkit starter has the skeleton only: `starter/capacitor.config.ts`, three files under `starter/packages/client/src/mobile/`, two dev shell scripts, a thin `starter/.github/workflows/mobile-release.yml`, and `build:mobile` defined as `pnpm build:client && cap sync`. No native trees, no build/verify script, no secret storage, no lifecycle or back-button model, no native CSS layer, and the export sets `assetPrefix: "./"`, which the reference had to delete because it 404s every route but `/` inside a Capacitor bundle. The CLI side is further along than the starter: `cli/src/scaffold/native-origins.ts`, `cli/src/scaffold/identifier-agreement.ts` and `cli/src/features/signing/native-rewriter.ts` already encode origin trust, identifier agreement and bundle-id rewriting, but none of that is wired into a gate the generated project runs on every build.

### Gaps

- [ ] **Make build-and-sync one scripted operation with preflights**
  - The starter defines the native build as `pnpm build:client && cap sync` (starter/package.json:37). Replace it with a single script that owns the whole sequence: resolve the platform set, run the preflights below, build the export, verify the artifact, then sync each platform. The two halves are only correct together, and the sync step copies whatever happens to be sitting in the configured output directory at that moment.
  - *Invariant:* No path may reach the native trees except through this script. A bare sync/run command copies the last thing written to the export directory, whatever wrote it.
  - *Reference:* `/Users/rico/projects/trackyourtime/scripts/build-mobile.mjs:1-38`
- [ ] **Give the native export its own output directory**
  - The starter points the native config at the same export directory the web build and the browser-test build write (starter/capacitor.config.ts:22 `webDir: "packages/client/out"`; starter/playwright.config.ts). Give the native build a distinct directory that nothing else ever writes, and point the native config at it.
  - *Invariant:* Exactly one writer per export directory. A shared directory means a test build — carrying a throwaway loopback backend origin — can be installed as the shipped app, with no error anywhere.
  - *Reference:* `/Users/rico/projects/trackyourtime/capacitor.config.ts:10-17`
- [ ] **Require the backend origin at build time and prove it landed in a chunk**
  - The starter passes the backend origin as workflow env only (starter/.github/workflows/mobile-release.yml:35-37) and the export throws if it is unset (starter/packages/client/next.config.ts:14-26), but nothing checks the value reached the emitted bundle. Require it in the build script, reject a value carrying a path, and after the build grep the emitted chunks for the literal.
  - *Invariant:* An inlined build-time origin that is empty or misspelled produces a green build and an app that resolves every request against its own document origin — which fails only on device. The literal must be proven present in an emitted chunk, not assumed from the environment.
  - *Reference:* `/Users/rico/projects/trackyourtime/scripts/build-mobile.mjs:113-137, 474-489`
- [ ] **Remove the relative asset prefix from the native export and assert it stays gone**
  - The starter's export config sets `assetPrefix: "./"` for every static export (starter/packages/client/next.config.ts:36), which the reference deleted. Add an export assertion that no emitted HTML file references a relative asset prefix.
  - *Invariant:* A native shell resolves assets from the bundle root, so a relative prefix 404s every route but the index. The failure is per-route and invisible on the first screen.
  - *Reference:* `/Users/rico/projects/trackyourtime/scripts/build-mobile.mjs:459-471`
- [ ] **Commit the generated native trees and say what inside them is regenerated**
  - The starter ships no native trees and deletes them on scaffold when the feature is off (cli/src/scaffold/app.ts:384-390). Generate them once at scaffold time, commit them, and document the three classes of file: hand-edited (platform config, transport exceptions, orientation, versions, signing), generated-but-tracked (dependency manifests regenerated by every sync), and untracked (the copied web assets).
  - *Invariant:* Every hand-edit the app needs lives only in the native trees, and the release workflow assumes they exist without ever running the generator. A checkout that has never run the build cannot open in the native IDEs — build first.
  - *Reference:* `/Users/rico/projects/trackyourtime/CLAUDE.md:421-447`
- [ ] **Decide the platform set from the toolchain, not from the directory existing**
  - With both trees committed, every checkout has both directories whether or not the machine can build them. Probe each toolchain; treat a platform named on the command line as a demand (missing toolchain is an error) and an auto-detected one as an offer (missing toolchain is skipped with a note).
  - *Invariant:* A bare build on a machine missing one platform's toolchain must not fail on the half the caller never asked for — otherwise CI runners and developer machines need different commands.
  - *Reference:* `/Users/rico/projects/trackyourtime/scripts/build-mobile.mjs:150-229`
- [ ] **Assert identifier agreement across every native copy, on every build**
  - The CLI already knows how to read and compare these (cli/src/scaffold/identifier-agreement.ts:1-287) and how to rewrite them (cli/src/features/signing/native-rewriter.ts:100-120), but the generated project runs no check. Wire the same comparison into the native build: bundle identifier in every build configuration, launcher label in the platform display-name key and in both string resources, and marketing version against the repo version.
  - *Invariant:* The scaffold writes these once; the generator never revisits them and the sync never rewrites them. A later change to the shared config silently does not reach the native trees, and the first symptom is a store rejecting an upload days later with a message that does not name the file.
  - *Reference:* `/Users/rico/projects/trackyourtime/scripts/build-mobile.mjs:307-410`
- [ ] **Preflight that every installed native plugin ships the package manifest the toolchain needs**
  - Walk the dependency list, select the packages that declare a native platform, and fail when one lacks the package manifest the platform's dependency manager requires.
  - *Invariant:* The sync drops a manifest-less plugin with a warning and still exits 0, so the plugin is simply absent on device after a green build. Anything depending on that plugin degrades silently — including the secret store.
  - *Reference:* `/Users/rico/projects/trackyourtime/scripts/build-mobile.mjs:20-24, 272-305`
- [ ] **Refuse the native build while a dev server owns the shared build directory**
  - Detect a live dev-server lock for the same checkout and fail with the two ways out (stop it, or give dev its own instance). Also note why: under a file-export configuration a custom output directory relocates the artifact but the internal build directory is forced back to the default.
  - *Invariant:* Two processes sharing the internal build directory either discard the dev cache or deadlock on the dev lock. Relocating the export does not relocate the build directory.
  - *Reference:* `/Users/rico/projects/trackyourtime/scripts/build-mobile.mjs:234-262`
- [ ] **Freeze the WebView scheme as a contract and document why**
  - Never set a custom scheme for either platform. Record in the generated project's memory file that the two default document origins are the app's real origins, that they differ per platform, and that both must be in the server's trust list.
  - *Invariant:* The scheme IS the document origin. Changing it later orphans every origin-scoped store on device at once and invalidates every trust-list entry — with no migration path, because the old origin is gone.
  - *Reference:* `/Users/rico/projects/trackyourtime/CLAUDE.md:449-453`
- [ ] **Ship debug-only cleartext exceptions, scoped to loopback**
  - Add a debug-source-set network config permitting cleartext to loopback and the emulator's host alias on one platform, and a loopback-scoped transport-security exception on the other. Both must be absent from release builds.

Release builds must keep the platform default — a global exception shipped to users is the quiet failure.

Replace it with three explicit tiers: the host secret store for credentials, the app-container key/value store for data that must survive, and web storage for the disposable. Implement the durable tier behind the project's existing key/value interface, with a one-time per-key hand-over from web storage and a per-key migration marker.

### Identifiers this area freezes

Every name below becomes a contract the first time this area ships. All of
them must come from the manifest's `identifiers` block — see
`cli/src/scaffold/identifiers.ts`. An area that spells one of these out as a
literal has re-introduced the divergence that block exists to prevent.

- Bundle identifier — the native bundle id in every build configuration of the iOS project file, the Android `namespace` and `applicationId`, the Capacitor `appId`, and the Electron build block. Registered with Apple and with Google Play; an App ID, an App Store Connect record and a Play package name cannot be renamed afterwards (reference: CLAUDE.md:22-27).
- Home-screen label (the SHORT form, not the product name) — web manifest `short_name`, Capacitor `appName`, iOS `CFBundleDisplayName`, Android `app_name` AND `title_activity_main`. Truncated at ~15 characters under an icon (CLAUDE.md:8-12; capacitor.config.ts:25-29).
- The WebView document origins — one per platform, fixed by never setting a custom scheme. They key every origin-scoped store on device and every entry in the server's trusted-origins list (CLAUDE.md:449-453; cli/src/scaffold/native-origins.ts:34-37).
- The secret-store item key holding the session credential (reference: `<slug>.session-token`, native-session.ts:50).
- The fresh-install marker key in the app-container store (reference: `<slug>.installed`, native-session.ts:56).
- Durable key/value store keys — the pending-write queue, its owner key, the session-scoped mirror key, and the per-key migration markers. Changing the address IS the data loss (preferences-storage.ts:32-58; running-mirror.ts:44).
- The client-kind string this shell sends on every request and uses as its device-flow client id (shell.ts:53-59).
- The custom URL scheme string in the Android string resources (cli/src/scaffold/identifier-agreement.ts:210).
- The signing team identifier in the iOS project file and the committed export-options file (ios/App/ExportOptions.plist:14).
- The Android signing environment variable names, which the build file, the workflow and the ignore rules must all agree on (android/app/build.gradle:22-26).

### Domain-specific — do not extract

- The three-tab bottom navigation and the specific destinations behind it (Track / Reports / More) — the generic part is 'phone-only chrome ships in the web bundle, is hidden by CSS, and derives its destinations from the existing route list'.
- The running timer itself, `entries.current`, elapsed-seconds derivation from an entry start, and `useRunningEntry` — generalised to 'one session-scoped authoritative value, mirrored to durable storage, seeded at boot, replaced only by a real success'.
- The tracker-bar composer, the timesheet grid writes, and `use-entry-mutations` — the generic residue is 'the writes the offline queue owns run in an always-network mode; the other mutations keep the default pause behaviour'.
- The runaway-timer local notification at start + maxHours, and the haptics on timer start/stop (docs/mobile-app-plan.md:429).
- The calendar touch-gesture fixes and the 7-column grid / ~980px report tables used as the justification for portrait-only orientation (ios/App/App/Info.plist:47-52) — the orientation lock itself is generic, the reason is not.
- Raycast's `toggle-timer` and the shared `decideTimerToggle` rule.
- Invoicing, e-invoice validation, projects/clients and the entry `source` enum values (`mobile` / `desktop` / `web` are generic; what they are stamped on is not).
- The product's own name, slug, vendor org and Apple team, the sibling bundle id `com.ricoslabs.raptorrunner`, and the live hosts `api.trackyourtime.dev` / `tracktime.trebeljahr.com`.
- The `prod:ios` / `prod:android` convention of baking the live production API into a visible Simulator/emulator run 'for a person checking the real apps' — the generic rule is 'a build that writes to production takes focus and is never run by agents or tests'; the specific host is not.

### Reference evidence

- One script that builds the export and syncs the native trees as a single operation, with a documented rationale for each preflight — `/Users/rico/projects/trackyourtime/scripts/build-mobile.mjs:1-38`
- Required baked backend origin, rejected if it carries a path, then verified to actually appear in an emitted chunk after the build — `/Users/rico/projects/trackyourtime/scripts/build-mobile.mjs:113-137, 474-489`
- Dedicated export directory for the native bundle so a browser-test build can never be installed as the app — `/Users/rico/projects/trackyourtime/scripts/build-mobile.mjs:11-20; /Users/rico/projects/trackyourtime/capacitor.config.ts:10-17`
- Export assertion that no emitted HTML uses a relative asset prefix — `/Users/rico/projects/trackyourtime/scripts/build-mobile.mjs:459-471`
