<a id="cross-platform-dev-infra"></a>

## Cross-platform dev infra

**Key:** `cross-platform-dev-infra` · **Starter today:** partial · **Open rows:** 28

The reference turns one vector mark into every shipped raster via `scripts/icons-brand.mjs` (25 targets: web favicon/apple-icon, extension icons, desktop `build/icon.png`, tray template + colour icons, store tiles, native launcher/splash/adaptive-layer sources, docs OG card), then fans those out with `icons-desktop.mjs` and `capacitor-assets`. Each native target gets its OWN export directory and a build script (`build-desktop.mjs`, `build-mobile.mjs`) that requires the build-time API origin, greps the emitted chunks for the literal, refuses to run beside a dev server, asserts native identifiers/versions against the root manifest, and verifies the packaged archive's contents and internal version. `scripts/dev.mjs` (608 lines) derives a per-checkout instance id that isolates ports, database and dist dir, pins ports for the main checkout and auto-picks in worktrees, derives shell/extension origins into the server's trust list, and runs the child tree as a process group with a reaper. CI is a channel-keyed matrix with per-channel secret scoping that fails closed. The starter has the shapes but not the mechanisms: a 116-line `dev.mjs` with random ports and no instance identity, `icons-desktop.mjs` (icns/ico only) fed by a hand-placed placeholder `build/icon.png`, no brand-source generator, `build:mobile` = `pnpm build:client && cap sync` into the SHARED `packages/client/out`, Electron loading `file://`, no version stamping into native projects, and a 64-line desktop-release workflow keyed by OS with no signing logic.

### Gaps

- [ ] **Generate every shipped bitmap from one vector source**
  - The starter ships hand-placed placeholder PNGs (build/icon.png 512x512, resources/icon.png 1024x1024, resources/splash.png 2732x2732, all committed binaries with no generator) and only an icns/ico converter. Add a single generator that reads one committed vector mark and emits every raster the project ships: browser/toolbar icon sizes, the desktop packager's source PNG, the native launcher-icon source, splash grounds, tray images, store tiles, the web favicon container and the touch icon. Each target in the table carries a comment saying which consumer asks for it and at what size. Render by converting the target pixel size into a density relative to the vector's own viewBox, supersampling ~4x and downsampling, so curved edges land on antialiased pixels.
  - *Invariant:* Every committed bitmap must be reproducible from the one vector source by running one command. A hand-edited binary has no source, cannot be re-derived when the mark changes, and drifts silently — the reference measured exactly this: before the generator existed, every shipped PNG but one was the starter's flat coloured placeholder square with no mark in it, inherited by the desktop, launcher and command-bar builds in turn.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/icons-brand.mjs:1-30, 80-105`
- [ ] **Commit the generated rasters, and say why**
  - The generated bitmaps are committed rather than built on demand, because the downstream toolchains cannot rasterize a vector themselves: the browser-extension manifest key takes PNG only, the native asset generator takes a PNG source, and the command-bar host reads PNG from its assets directory. The generator's header must state this, or a later reader deletes them as build output.
  - *Invariant:* A generated artifact that a third-party toolchain consumes directly must be committed AND must carry a comment naming its generator. Without the comment it gets hand-edited; without the commit the third-party build breaks on a fresh checkout.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/icons-brand.mjs:17-21`
- [ ] **Emit the optional variant inputs the native asset generator silently skips**
  - The native asset generator treats the dark splash and the two adaptive-icon layers as OPTIONAL inputs: absent, it produces no dark variant and falls back to a flat legacy icon, with no error. The starter's resources/ holds only icon.png and splash.png, so both fallbacks are active. Emit the full input set: light splash, dark splash, adaptive foreground (mark on transparency) and adaptive background (flat brand ground).
  - *Invariant:* An absent optional input to an asset generator is not an error — it is a wrong-looking default that ships. A dark-mode device launches into a full-screen white rectangle before the pre-paint theme script runs, and the launcher shows a square icon among rounded ones.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/icons-brand.mjs:120-152`
- [ ] **Tie the splash ground colour to the shell's own background constant**
  - The splash background colours passed to the native asset generator and the WebView/shell background colour in the platform config must be the same two values, defined once and referenced from both. The starter passes a literal '#ffffff' to the generator and sets backgroundColor '#ffffff' in the platform config independently, with no link between them and no dark counterpart.
  - *Invariant:* A splash that hands over to a differently coloured shell surface is a visible seam at every cold start. The two values are one decision, so they must have one definition.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/icons-brand.mjs:45-55, capacitor.config.ts`
- [ ] **Emit platform-correct tray/menu-bar images, not one image reused**
  - macOS takes a TEMPLATE image — black on transparent, tinted by the OS for light and dark — identified by a `Template` filename suffix, with `@2x` picked up for retina. Other desktop platforms draw in colour on backgrounds nobody controls and take the full-colour tile, plus a platform-native icon container for Windows and two specific pixel sizes for Linux indicator areas. The starter emits none of these.
  - *Invariant:* The platform decides the icon colour model. A coloured image where a template is expected has its colour stripped by the OS and renders as a smear; a bare monochrome mark where colour is expected disappears against a themed background.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/icons-brand.mjs:196-245`
- [ ] **Emit store-package tile assets for any store packaging target**
  - The store packaging target reads a fixed set of named tile images from the packager's build-resources directory. The starter has no such directory content, so the packager substitutes its own samples.
  - *Invariant:* Absent tile assets do not fail the package — the packager silently ships its own sample tiles, which is a grey placeholder square on the Start menu of every store install.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/icons-brand.mjs:247-266`
- [ ] **One version source plus a test that asserts every literal copy**
  - The root manifest's version is the single source of truth. Build-time-readable consumers (the web export, the extension) must READ it; everything that cannot (native project files, in-source constants, self-host compose image-tag defaults, example env files) keeps a literal copy, and a test asserts each copy against the root and names the file that drifted. The starter has no such test and no native version stamping at all.
  - *Invariant:* A drifted version copy ships a client that reports the wrong version in its handshake and in its own settings screen, with nothing failing anywhere. The test is the only thing that turns that into a loud failure, at the first test run after a release bump that missed one.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/lib/version-sync.test.mjs:1-20, 44-78`
- [ ] **Assert the packaged artifact's internal version, and the tag, at build time**
  - After packaging, extract the manifest from inside each freshly written archive and compare its version to the root; on macOS also read the bundle plist's short version string. On a tag-triggered run, check before anything is built that the tag equals the root version. Restrict the check to artifacts this run wrote (mtime), so a stale output directory neither passes for nor fails this build.
  - *Invariant:* A release named X whose packaged manifest reports Y is never offered its own update by the update feed — the feed and the installed build disagree forever, and no step fails. Check the tag BEFORE the export, so a typo costs seconds and not an hour of matrix time.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/build-desktop.mjs:44-47, 131-136, 356-380`
- [ ] **Give every build target its own export directory**
  - The starter's desktop packager, mobile sync and end-to-end test harness all read `packages/client/out`. Give each target its own directory — one per shell, written only by that shell's build script — and point the platform config's web directory at the shell's own. Write a small marker file beside the export recording what origin and timestamp it was built against.
  - *Invariant:* A shared output directory means the last build wins silently. The test harness bakes a throwaway loopback backend origin into its export; a subsequent shell build (or an implicit sync inside a `run` command) then installs an app permanently pointed at a dead port, with no error anywhere.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/build-mobile.mjs:6-16, 46-49; scripts/build-desktop.mjs:31-37`
- [ ] **Require the build-time backend origin, then prove it reached the bundle**
  - Each shell build script must refuse to run when the public backend-origin variable is unset, validate it parses as a URL whose path is '/' with no query or fragment, and AFTER the build grep the emitted JavaScript chunks for that literal. Fail with the exact two commands the developer needs (pin the dev ports, then build against them). The starter passes the value straight through from a CI secret with no check.
  - *Invariant:* A build-time public variable that is unset does not fail the build — the client falls back to its own document origin (the shell's custom scheme or loopback), every request fails on device, and the build stays green. Requiring the variable is not enough: it can be set and still not reach the bundle, so the emitted artifact must be searched for the literal.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/build-mobile.mjs:110-148, 470-500; scripts/build-desktop.mjs:160-185, 250-270`
- [ ] **Refuse a relative asset prefix in the emitted HTML**
  - After the export, walk every emitted HTML file and fail if any references the framework's asset directory relatively. Name the offending files and the likely cause (an asset-prefix setting in the framework config).
  - *Invariant:* A shell serves its bundle from a root-having origin, so a relative asset prefix 404s every chunk on every route except the index — which is the one route a smoke test usually opens. The failure is invisible in the build and in the happy path.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/build-desktop.mjs:38-41, 236-250`
- [ ] **Refuse to build beside a running dev server of the same checkout**
  - Before any production build in the same checkout, read the framework's dev lock file, probe its recorded pid with a zero signal, and refuse with the kill command and the per-instance escape hatch. The reference does this in three places: the dev script itself, the desktop build and the mobile build.
  - *Invariant:* Under a static-export configuration a custom output directory only moves the EXPORT — the framework still uses its default internal build directory — so a production build and a dev server of the same checkout share it and corrupt one another, or deadlock on the dev lock. The dev server is also detached and can outlive the terminal that started it.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/build-mobile.mjs:24-32, 240-270; scripts/dev.mjs:255-295`
- [ ] **Derive a per-checkout instance identity for dev**
  - Hash the checkout's absolute path into a sanitised id and use it to name the dev database and (when explicitly requested) the framework dist directory. Sanitise for the strictest consumer's rules. Document why the dist directory is NOT per-instance by default: the framework rewrites the dist path into tracked config files, so a per-instance default leaves them permanently dirty and two instances fight over them.
  - *Invariant:* Two checkouts of one project running at once must never share mutable local state. A shared dev database means one agent's fixtures clobber another's; a shared dist directory means two build caches fight. The id must be stable across restarts of the same checkout and distinct between checkouts, so derive it from the path, not from a clock or a random value.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/dev.mjs:73-95, 224-250`
- [ ] **Two dev port modes, and auto-detect the worktree case**
  - Pin the ports by default with named constants in one place; add an auto mode that picks from the ephemeral range; make a linked worktree behave like auto automatically (detected by git-dir differing from git-common-dir), with an explicit fixed flag that overrides even there. A busy pinned port warns and falls back for that run; under the explicit fixed flag it exits instead, printing the command that shows who holds the port. The starter picks random ports in three fixed sub-ranges with no instance awareness and no worktree handling.
  - *Invariant:* Every client that is not the browser bakes or stores the backend origin — so a port that moves per run breaks each of them, and it also breaks password managers, saved logins and redirect allowlists that key on the client origin. But pinned ports collide when several checkouts run at once. The two callers want opposite things, so both modes must exist and the worktree case must pick the right one without anyone remembering to.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/dev.mjs:92-148, 178-215; CLAUDE.md:96-122`
- [ ] **One canonical browser-facing host for every derived URL**
  - Define one host constant (overridable by env) and use it for every printed URL, the client's backend and socket URLs, the frontend URL passed to the server and the auth base URL. The starter hardcodes 127.0.0.1 in some places and localhost in others (dev.mjs prints 127.0.0.1; dev:desktop waits on localhost:3000).
  - *Invariant:* localhost and 127.0.0.1 are different origins AND different sites. Mixing them breaks CORS and stops SameSite session cookies from being sent to the backend — and the symptom is an intermittent 'not signed in', not a CORS error anyone reads.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/dev.mjs:64-71`
- [ ] **Derive shell and extension origins in the dev script and pass them on the child's command line**
  - The dev script should derive the unpacked-extension origin from the hash of its dist path (the same hash the browser uses), list the packaged shells' document origins, merge anything already in the environment, and pass the whole list to the server child as a command-line-prefixed variable — not via the env file.
  - *Invariant:* The env-file loader does not override a key already present in the real environment, so a value placed only in the env file is silently skipped when the parent also sets it. And a shell's document origin is cross-site to the backend, so an origin missing from the trust list produces a 403 before credentials are even checked, plus a refused socket upgrade — which reads as 'the app is offline' against a server that is up.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/dev.mjs:297-370, 400-412`
- [ ] **Run the dev child tree as a process group with a reaper**
  - Spawn the concurrent runner detached as its own process group leader, forward SIGINT/SIGTERM/SIGHUP to the group, treat a broken stdout pipe and being reparented as a hangup, and start a small reaper process holding a stdin pipe so a SIGKILL of the parent still takes the group down. Pipe (do not inherit) child output so watcher-exhaustion errors can be recognised and explained. Use a kill-others-ON-FAIL policy, not kill-others: a child exiting zero must not tear down the rest. The starter uses `execSync('npx concurrently -k ...')`, which is exactly the shape the reference replaced.
  - *Invariant:* A signal aimed at the launcher's pid alone orphans the whole watcher tree, which is then reparented and runs for days holding thousands of file descriptors — until a fresh dev server can open no watches and answers 404 on EVERY route while static files still load. Never kill by command-line pattern to clean this up (that hits other checkouts); kill the group you started.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/dev.mjs:429-500; CLAUDE.md:132-150`
- [ ] **Probe toolchains to decide which native platforms to act on**
  - Both native project trees are committed, so their presence proves nothing about the machine. Probe each platform's toolchain (SDK selected, an emulator/simulator available, a runtime present at the same paths the dev shell scripts use). A platform named explicitly on the command line is a demand — a missing toolchain is an error; an auto-detected one is an offer — a missing toolchain skips it with a note telling the caller how to make it an error.
  - *Invariant:* Directory existence is not capability. Without this split, a bare build fails on the half of the matrix the caller never asked for: a machine with only one platform's SDK, or a CI runner that builds only one target, cannot run the default command at all.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/build-mobile.mjs:149-235; CLAUDE.md:502-509`
- [ ] **Assert native-project identifiers and versions against the source of truth before syncing**
  - Before syncing into the native trees, read each native project file and assert: no template placeholder identifier survives; every build configuration's bundle identifier equals the platform config's app id; the marketing version and the Android version name equal the root manifest's version; the display name and both launcher label strings equal the platform config's app name. Fail naming the exact file and telling the caller that every build configuration in it must be edited.
  - *Invariant:* The native project generator writes these identifiers ONCE, at add time, and the sync command never revisits them — so any later change to the platform config or the root version silently does not reach the native trees. The drift is invisible until a store rejects the upload or a released build reports the wrong version.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/build-mobile.mjs:300-420; CLAUDE.md:427-438`
- [ ] **Verify the packaged archive's contents against an allowlist**
  - After packaging, list the archive's entries and fail on anything outside an explicit allowlist (the compiled main bundle, the export, the manifest), with a separate named check for the runtime dependency families the root manifest would otherwise drag in. Only inspect artifacts this run wrote.
  - *Invariant:* The packager's default file set pulls the root manifest's runtime dependencies into the archive. That does not fail — it just ships tens of megabytes of another platform's native modules inside the desktop app. An allowlist plus a named check for the known offender is what keeps the package honest.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/build-desktop.mjs:41-44, 336-356`
- [ ] **Key the release matrix on distribution channel, not on OS, and scope secrets per leg**
  - Replace the OS-keyed three-leg matrix with one leg per distribution channel, each carrying its own packager arguments and artifact globs (two channels can share one OS runner; one architecture may need its own runner because its packaging tool cannot cross-build). Resolve each leg's credentials through matrix-conditional expressions at JOB level, so a step's condition can read them and each leg sees only its own certificate. Signing fails closed: a complete secret set signs and the signature is verified, no secrets produce artifacts whose FILENAME says unsigned, and a partial set fails the leg before the export is built.
  - *Invariant:* Two channels on one OS use different certificates. If both are visible to both legs, the packager picks up whichever it finds and signs the wrong artifact. And a partially configured channel must fail loudly before the build: the in-between state otherwise produces a plausible-looking artifact that no store and no updater accepts. Name the artifact for its signing state so an unsigned build can never be mistaken for a release download.
  - *Reference:* `.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml:12-19, 86-172`
- [ ] **Validate free-text release inputs in a cheap job before the matrix**
  - Put a seconds-long validation job ahead of the expensive build matrix for any dispatch input or repo variable that steers the release, and pass the free text to the script through the ENVIRONMENT, never interpolated into the shell command.
  - *Invariant:* A typo in a release input that is only read at the publish step costs the whole matrix before it is caught. And a workflow input is attacker-controllable free text: interpolating it into a run block is command injection.
  - *Reference:* `.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml:63-85`
- [ ] **Source build-time public values from repo variables with literal fallbacks, never from secrets**
  - Every value inlined into a client bundle at build time should come from a repo VARIABLE with a literal fallback expression, plus a notice step that states which optional integration this build has off. The starter reads the backend origin from `secrets.NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` in both the desktop and mobile release workflows, and from unset repo variables with no fallback in the deploy workflow.
  - *Invariant:* An unset secret expands to the empty string with no warning, so every build silently ships a client that talks to no backend — the reference hit exactly this with unset repo variables and added the literal fallback because of it. A build-time public value is not a secret; it is in the shipped bundle by definition. And an artifact that reports nothing should say so in the log, not look like one that does.
  - *Reference:* `.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml:49-60; .github/workflows/build-and-deploy.yml:243-297`
- [ ] **Stamp the deployed artifact with its commit and version, and serialise it safely**
  - Write a small static version file next to the web export carrying the build commit, the release version and the baked backend origin, serialised with a JSON serialiser rather than printf so no value can break the quoting. CI polls it after a deploy, and open browser tabs compare it against the commit baked into their own bundle to offer a reload. The CLI already scaffolds the commit+origin pair; add the release version.
  - *Invariant:* A deployed artifact must be able to answer what it is. Without the stamp, a deploy that silently served the previous image is indistinguishable from a successful one, and a long-open tab never learns it is stale.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/write-version-json.mjs:1-33; cli/src/scaffold/deploy-verification.ts:169-177`
- [ ] **Preflight a dependency whose binary arrives lazily, and pin the runtime that extracts it correctly**
  - For any dependency that downloads its own large binary on first require rather than in an install script, add a preflight that checks the extracted state (marker file present, recorded version matching the package version, executable present) and downloads it explicitly. Call it from the build, the end-to-end harness and CI. Pin the runtime version in a version file and say why in a comment.
  - *Invariant:* Exit code zero is not proof of a complete extraction — a newer runtime stopped the extraction partway and still exited zero, leaving a truncated binary and no marker file. Inspect the extracted state, do not trust the exit code. And adding such a package to the package manager's build allowlist does nothing, because there is no install script to allow.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/ensure-electron.mjs:1-45`
- [ ] **Ship project-agnostic cross-platform try-it tooling**
  - Two tools, neither importing from the project around it: a drop tool that copies an unpacked build (or a single installer file) into one shared folder next to a generated double-click launcher, writing a small record of the commit, the dirty state and what was baked in; and a headless smoke tool that runs the built app in a container under a virtual display, drives it over the debug protocol and checks four things — the app origin loads, no uncaught error, a screenshot succeeds, and it is still alive some seconds later. The reference author marked this folder as intended to move into the scaffolder.
  - *Invariant:* The launcher must mirror the drop to the guest's own disk before running it: a network share client refuses files over a size limit that a desktop shell binary exceeds, and an app running from the share locks its own files so the next drop cannot replace them. Swap each drop in whole so a guest never sees a mix of two builds. And on a virtual display the window must be SHOWN — an unmapped window has no surface to copy from, so the screenshot hangs until the caller times out.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/crossplat/README.md:1-60; docs/cross-platform-testing.md:60-90; CLAUDE.md:234-252`
- [ ] **Never open a window on the host screen in an agent or CI run**
  - Split the build script's 'open it afterwards' behaviour behind an explicit flag documented as for people, never agents, and validate that it is only passed together with an unpacked package. Give the shell a headless environment flag that never shows or focuses a window and, on macOS, uses the accessory activation policy so no dock icon appears. Keep the same split for VM-launch helpers (the option that starts a VM opens a GUI window, so it stays off in agent and CI runs).
  - *Invariant:* A build that launches its own window steals focus from whoever is using the machine, and does so on every iteration. The headless contract — never shown, never focused, no dock icon — must be asserted by a test on every platform, or it regresses the first time someone debugs a window-visibility problem.
  - *Reference:* `scripts/build-desktop.mjs:76-82, 400-425; CLAUDE.md:234-252; scripts/crossplat/README.md:40-50`
- [ ] **Document the generator churn in tracked native files**
  - The native asset generator and the sync command both rewrite tracked files in the native project trees (a package-manifest file regenerated on every sync, and unrelated project-file keys the asset generator reformats). Note which diffs are expected, which should be committed (when the plugin set changed) and which should be reverted, and have the build script print that note when it sees the diff.
  - *Invariant:* Undocumented generator churn gets stashed away or committed blindly. One of these files hardcodes the package manager's content-addressed store paths, so any lockfile refresh renames those directories and the committed file points at paths that no longer exist — dependency resolution then fails on a checkout that has never built.
  - *Reference:* `CLAUDE.md:440-445, 488-497; scripts/build-mobile.mjs:520-535`

### Identifiers this area freezes

Every name below becomes a contract the first time this area ships. All of
them must come from the manifest's `identifiers` block — see
`cli/src/scaffold/identifiers.ts`. An area that spells one of these out as a
literal has re-introduced the divergence that block exists to prevent.

- The packaged desktop shell's custom scheme + host (it keys browser-side storage, must appear verbatim in the server trust list, and appears in the dev script's derived origin list and the build script's archive allowlist paths)
- The per-target export directory names (one per shell; the platform config's web directory points at the mobile one, the packager's file list at the desktop one, and the archive allowlist checks those literal paths)
- The reverse-DNS application identifier, asserted by the mobile build against every native build configuration
- The launcher label, asserted against the native display-name key and both launcher label strings
- The build-time public env var prefix (framework-specific, e.g. NEXT_PUBLIC_*) and the exact names of the injected variables — they appear in the Dockerfile ARG/ENV pairs, the CI build-args block, the dev script's child env strings and each shell build script's required-variable check
- The project env-var prefix used for headless/profile/user-data-dir overrides in the shell and its test harness
- The desktop profile/user-data directory name (packaged vs unpackaged variants), pinned by name rather than derived from the package manifest
- The brand colour constants: the tile/adaptive-icon ground, and the light and dark splash grounds — the dark one must equal the shell's WebView background constant
- The tray/menu-bar image filenames and their platform suffixes (the Template suffix and @2x are read by the OS, not by the app)
- The store-package tile filenames and their exact pixel sizes, which the packager maps by name
- The pinned dev ports (client/API/docs) — the extension manifest config and the command-bar preference defaults must follow them
- The version file path served by the deployed web artifact (/version.json) and its key names (commit, version, apiUrl), which CI polling and the stale-tab check both read
- The root manifest's version field as the single version source, and the tag prefix convention (v<version>) the build script asserts against

### Domain-specific — do not extract

- The tray icon's two states (idle ring vs closed ring / red dot badge) express whether a time tracker's timer is running — the generic form is 'the shell's status icon has a small set of states mirroring one piece of session state', but the specific idle/running artwork and the taskbar overlay badge are domain
- The command-bar host's menu-bar image, sized for a persistent timer readout in the macOS menu bar
- The shared toggle rule between the desktop tray, the command-bar extension and the web app (decideTimerToggle) — it is a domain decision function, not build infra
- The e-invoice validation workflow (.github/workflows/einvoice-validate.yml) and its Mustang/veraPDF validators — an entirely domain-specific CI leg
- The newsletter scripts (scripts/newsletter-*.ts) in the root scripts directory
- The API-level handshake constants and header names (x-<product>-client-version / x-<product>-api-level) — surveyed here only because the version-copies test asserts them; they belong to a client/server compatibility area, not to build infra
- The self-host compose image-tag default assertions in the version test, which encode this product's two-image self-hosting topology
- The specific brand indigo (#4F46E5) and the specific vector mark files (mark-tile.svg, mark-adaptive-foreground.svg, social-card.svg)
- The docs-site Open Graph card generation, which is specific to this project's Docusaurus docs site

### Reference evidence

- One vector mark rasterized into 25 committed bitmap targets, each with the reason it exists (browser toolbar sizes, desktop packager input, launcher-icon source, tray template images, store tiles, OG card, favicon.ico assembled from PNG entries in-process) — `scripts/icons-brand.mjs:1-260`
- Header states the failure mode that motivated the script: before it existed every shipped PNG except one was a flat #6366F1 square with no mark in it — the starter placeholder, inherited by the desktop, Raycast and mobile builds in turn — `scripts/icons-brand.mjs:11-16`
- Supersample-then-downsample render helper: target pixel size converted to an SVG density relative to a 72 DPI baseline, rendered at 4x, resized with transparent background — `scripts/icons-brand.mjs:39-70`
- Splash composite with light and dark grounds; the dark ground constant is documented as having to equal the WebView background the shell hands over to, or the seam is visible — `scripts/icons-brand.mjs:45-55, 110-135`
- Android adaptive-icon foreground/background emitted as two separate layers, because the launcher crops and parallaxes them independently and a baked-in mark slides out of frame under the mask; absent, the generator falls back to a legacy square icon — `scripts/icons-brand.mjs:137-152`
- macOS tray images are monochrome-on-transparent template images with `Template` and `@2x` filename conventions; Windows/Linux tray images use the colour tile because they draw on backgrounds nobody controls — `scripts/icons-brand.mjs:196-245`
- Store-package tiles generated because, absent, the packager silently ships its own sample tiles — a grey placeholder square on every Start menu install — `scripts/icons-brand.mjs:247-266`
- Downstream fan-out ordering documented in the header: brand source -> icons:desktop (icns/ico) -> mobile:assets (native icon/splash sets) — `scripts/icons-brand.mjs:22-29`
- icns/ico generation from the single PNG; Linux consumes the PNG directly via the packager config, so only the two platform container formats are emitted — `scripts/icons-desktop.mjs:1-25`
- CLAUDE.md: `build/icon.png` is generated — re-derive it from the mark, then fan it out. Do not hand-edit it. — `CLAUDE.md:186-189`
- Version copies test: root manifest version is the single source; every workspace manifest, every literal copy in source constants, the native iOS MARKETING_VERSION and Android versionName, and the self-host compose image-tag defaults are all asserted against it, with the reason (a drifted copy ships a client reporting the wrong version with nothing failing anywhere) — `scripts/lib/version-sync.test.mjs:1-88`
- Test also asserts that the web export and the extension READ the root version at build time rather than keeping a copy (by matching the config source) — `scripts/lib/version-sync.test.mjs:79-87`
- Deploy version stamp file: commit, version and the baked API origin serialised with JSON.stringify so no value can break the quoting; read by CI after deploy and by open browser tabs to offer a reload — `scripts/write-version-json.mjs:1-33`
- Desktop build script: own export dir, required build-time API origin, literal grepped out of emitted chunks, relative asset-prefix refusal, dev-server lock preflight, esbuild bundling of main+preload, tray-icon presence check, packaged-archive entry allowlist, packed version == root version, and macOS bundle plist version check — `scripts/build-desktop.mjs:1-60, 150-260, 300-380`
- Tag/version agreement checked before anything is built: a tag run must match the root manifest version, with the reason (a release named 0.2.0 that reports 0.1.0 would never be offered its own update) — `scripts/build-desktop.mjs:44-47, 131-136`
- Packaged archive contents asserted against an explicit allowlist, and specifically scanned for the mobile plugin packages the root dependencies would otherwise drag in — `scripts/build-desktop.mjs:336-356`
- Mobile build script header: the four reasons this is a script and not `build && sync` — shared output dir gets a test build's throwaway origin installed as the app, build-time origin must be verified in the bundle, the package manager silently drops plugins lacking a Swift package manifest and exits 0, and the framework forces the internal build dir back to the default under static export — `scripts/build-mobile.mjs:1-37`
- Toolchain probing decides the platform set: a platform named on the command line is a demand and a missing toolchain is an error; an auto-detected one is an offer and is skipped with a note — because both native trees are committed and exist on machines that cannot build them — `scripts/build-mobile.mjs:149-235`
- Native identifier and version agreement asserted for both platforms before syncing: template placeholder detection, every build configuration's bundle identifier, marketing version, display name, Android namespace/applicationId/versionName and both launcher label strings — `scripts/build-mobile.mjs:300-420`
- CLAUDE.md states why: the native project generator writes those identifiers ONCE at add time and `sync` never revisits them, so a later change to the config silently does not reach the native trees and is invisible until a store rejects the upload — `CLAUDE.md:427-438`
- Dev script derives a per-checkout instance id from the absolute path, and uses it to isolate the database name and optionally the framework dist dir — `scripts/dev.mjs:1-95, 224-250`
- Pinned vs auto port modes, with the reason spelled out: every client that is not the browser bakes or stores the API origin at build time, so a port that moves per run makes each of them a copy-paste chore; agents get random high ports — `scripts/dev.mjs:92-115, 178-215`
- Linked-worktree detection (git-dir vs git-common-dir) makes `dev` behave like `dev:auto` automatically so concurrent agents never fight over the pinned ports; an explicit --fixed still wins — `scripts/dev.mjs:116-148, 190-193`
- One canonical browser-facing host used for every printed URL and every derived origin, because localhost and 127.0.0.1 are different origins AND different sites — mixing them breaks CORS and stops SameSite cookies reaching the API — `scripts/dev.mjs:64-71`
- Framework dev-server lock preflight: reads the lock file, probes the pid with signal 0, refuses with the exact kill command and the isolation escape hatch — `scripts/dev.mjs:255-295`
- Dev script derives the unpacked-extension origin from the dist path hash and the packaged shells' origins, and passes them to the server child on the COMMAND LINE — because the env-file loader does not override a key already in the environment, so a value in the file would be skipped — `scripts/dev.mjs:297-370, 400-412`
- Child tree runs as its own process group with a reaper on a stdin pipe, covering SIGINT/SIGTERM/SIGHUP, EPIPE, reparenting and SIGKILL; output is piped so a watcher failure is explained rather than scrolling past — `scripts/dev.mjs:429-500`
- CLAUDE.md records the measured failure this fixed: orphaned watchers from earlier runs held thousands of file descriptors until a fresh dev server could open no watches and answered 404 on every route while static files still loaded — `CLAUDE.md:132-150`
- Binary-availability preflight for a dependency with no install script, which also detects a truncated extraction that still exits 0 under a newer runtime — hence the pinned runtime version file — `scripts/ensure-electron.mjs:1-45`
- Desktop release matrix keyed by DISTRIBUTION CHANNEL, not by OS: six legs, each with its own packager arguments and artifact globs, two of which share one OS runner — `.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml:86-145`
- Per-channel secret scoping at job level via matrix-conditional expressions, so each leg sees only its own certificate; signing fails closed (complete set signs, none produces files named -unsigned, partial set fails before the export) — `.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml:12-19, 146-172`
- A cheap validation job runs before the expensive matrix so a typo in a dispatch input fails in seconds rather than an hour later; the free-text input is passed through the environment, never interpolated into the script — `.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml:63-85`
- Build-time public values come from repo VARIABLES with a literal fallback, never secrets, with the reason: an unset secret expands to empty string and would ship an app that talks to no API — `.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml:49-60`
- Client image build args carry every build-time public value plus the commit, with a comment noting these are inlined at image BUILD time so setting them as runtime env on the container does nothing — `.github/workflows/build-and-deploy.yml:266-297`
- A notice step states which optional integration this build has off, so an artifact that reports nothing says so rather than looking like one that does — `.github/workflows/build-and-deploy.yml:243-256`
- Cross-platform try-it tooling that is explicitly project-agnostic and explicitly earmarked to move into hatchkit: a VM drop folder with a double-click launcher, and a containerised headless smoke run under a virtual display — `scripts/crossplat/README.md:1-60`
- Containerised smoke run checks four things (page at the app origin appears, no uncaught error, screenshot succeeds, still running 5 s later) and runs the window SHOWN on a virtual display, because an unmapped X11 window has no surface to copy and the capture hangs — `docs/cross-platform-testing.md:1-90, CLAUDE.md:234-252`
- Native asset generator also rewrites tracked native project files; the churn is documented as expected and to be reverted rather than committed — `CLAUDE.md:488-491`
