<a id="i18n"></a>

## i18n

**Key:** `i18n` · **Starter today:** absent · **Open rows:** 18

The reference ships a complete, framework-free i18n layer: one shared locale contract (`packages/shared/src/locale.ts`), type-checked ICU catalogs per namespace per locale with a hand-written ICU walker driving placeholder-parity tests and a runtime pseudo-locale (`packages/shared/src/i18n-catalog.ts`), an external-store locale state split into `target`/`current` so a statically prerendered page can hydrate in the source language and then switch before paint behind a CSS gate (`packages/client/src/i18n/locale-store.ts`, `app/pre-paint.ts`), a synced-preference/device-resolved model (`locale-sync.tsx`, `packages/server/src/services/user-locale.ts`), a separate server catalog for generated documents and outbound mail, per-language statically built public pages with hreflang, and a second client surface (browser extension) that reuses the account preference instead of the host platform's i18n API. The Hatchkit starter has none of this: no catalog, no locale module, no pre-paint script, no locale state, `<html lang="en">` is hardcoded in `starter/packages/client/src/app/layout.tsx:21`, the only formatting call is a bare `toLocaleString()` in `starter/packages/client/src/components/ml/result-viewer-3d.tsx:37`, and the synced profile preferences (`starter/packages/server/src/models/Profile.ts`) carry `theme` and `notifications` only. The starter also lacks the sibling pre-paint machinery (no theme script, no `suppressHydrationWarning` on `<html>`), which the locale gate is built on top of. The CLI does already have the one piece the gap depends on: a frozen scaffold-time `storagePrefix` identifier (`cli/src/scaffold/identifiers.ts:93-97`).

### Gaps

- [ ] **Add one shared locale contract module**
  - A single module in the shared package owns: the list of shipped locales (source language first), the default locale, the stored-preference union (`system` + each locale), type guards, a BCP-47 matcher that keys off the primary subtag only, a matcher over a preference-ordered tag list, and a resolver turning (preference, device tags) into a locale. Every other package imports it — client, server, and every secondary client.
  - *Invariant:* There is exactly one list of supported locales in the whole repo. A second copy is a language one surface accepts and another silently renders in the source language. Matching on the primary subtag only is deliberate: a regional tag whose catalog is not region-specific must still match, or regional users silently fall back to the source language.
  - *Reference:* `packages/shared/src/locale.ts:15-62`
- [ ] **Make translations type-checked against the source catalog**
  - One catalog file per namespace per locale. The source-language file is declared `as const` so its literal types drive key and argument checking; every other locale's file annotates its object literal directly with a mapped type that widens leaves to `string` while keeping the key set exact. One index file is the only place namespaces are listed. All locales are bundled statically, not lazily chunked.
  - *Invariant:* A missing, misspelled or extra key in a translation must fail the type-check, not render at runtime. The annotation must be on the object literal itself — assigning through an intermediate variable loses the excess-property check and extra keys stop failing. Lazy-loading a catalog breaks the pre-paint gate: the page stays hidden while a chunk loads.
  - *Reference:* `packages/client/src/i18n/messages/index.ts:1-67`
- [ ] **Add the ICU walker and the catalog parity test**
  - A small reader of the ICU subset in use (`{arg}`, typed args, plural/select/selectordinal, `#`, apostrophe quoting, rich-text tags) that can tell literal text from syntax without formatting anything. On top of it: a test asserting per message that every locale uses the same placeholder and tag names, that each message parses as ICU in its own locale, and that no multi-word source sentence survives verbatim in a translation.
  - *Invariant:* The type system cannot see inside a message string. A translation that renames a placeholder, drops a plural branch's `#`, or breaks ICU syntax type-checks perfectly and then renders a raw placeholder — or throws — in one language only, on one screen, for one audience. Only a string-level parity test catches it.
  - *Reference:* `packages/shared/src/i18n-catalog.ts:62-215`
- [ ] **Add a runtime pseudo-locale, dev builds only**
  - Derived from the source catalog at runtime by accenting every letter, padding ~35% in word-sized runs (not one unbroken run, which can never wrap), and bracketing — placeholders, selectors, `#` and tags left intact. Switched on by a query parameter that persists, by a second parameter that clears it, or from the settings picker. Built behind a build-mode flag so the production bundle contains no trace of it, and never sent to the server.
  - *Invariant:* Text that shows up unaccented was never extracted. Text that clips in the pseudo-locale will clip in any longer target language. Padding must break into word-sized runs or a long sentence is reported as overflowing when it would actually wrap. The pseudo-locale must be derived from the source, so it can never be missing a key.
  - *Reference:* `packages/shared/src/i18n-catalog.ts:217-300`
- [ ] **Use a framework-free translator core, not a routing-based i18n framework**
  - Bind an ICU formatter core directly to the locale state — no provider component, no request middleware. Memoise one translator per (locale, namespace). Missing messages render as `namespace.key` (visible, greppable) and log only in dev. Time zone resolves to the device's in a browser and to UTC during a build.
  - *Invariant:* A routing-based i18n framework needs request middleware, which a statically exported client cannot have. With no provider to mount, every existing component test keeps rendering unchanged in the source language. A missing message must never throw mid-render in production — a half-translated screen is recoverable, a crash is not.
  - *Reference:* `packages/client/src/i18n/translator.ts:20-97`
- [ ] **Provide a call-time translator for non-component code**
  - A `translate(namespace)` helper that reads the active locale at the moment it is called, for toasts fired from mutation callbacks, strings built in plain functions, generated filenames.
  - *Invariant:* Never call it at module scope. The locale switches after hydration, so a string computed at import time stays in the source language for the life of the tab — and nothing about that is visible in a test that never switches locale.
  - *Reference:* `packages/client/src/i18n/translate.ts:1-16`
- [ ] **Hold the rendered locale in an external store with a target/current split**
  - Two values: `target` (what the person wants — stored preference resolved against device languages, known at module load) and `current` (what the renderer has been told to render). `current` starts as the prerender language and only becomes `target` in a layout effect after hydration. The store's hook returns the prerender language as its server snapshot. Expose separate `set` (person's choice — stored, applied, published to a sink) and `adopt` (came from the server — applied but never echoed back) entry points, plus a reset for tests.
  - *Invariant:* Rendering the target language during hydration is a text mismatch on every string, which the renderer reports once in the console and then 'fixes' by discarding the served DOM. Returning the prerender language from the server snapshot makes that impossible by construction rather than by discipline. Echoing a server-supplied preference back to the sink writes the server's own answer to it on every refetch.
  - *Reference:* `packages/client/src/i18n/locale-store.ts:23-243`
- [ ] **Add the pre-paint resolve-and-gate script**
  - An inlined head script that resolves the language exactly as the store does, writes the language attribute and a data attribute on the document element, and — only when the answer differs from the prerender language — sets a pending attribute. Unlayered CSS hides the gated app subtree via `visibility` while that attribute is present; a descendant marker sets it back to visible for subtrees that own their language outright. A layout effect removes the attribute once the switch commits, and a several-second timer removes it unconditionally as a failsafe. A test must run the real script string and the store resolver over the same inputs.
  - *Invariant:* The script and the store must resolve identically or the gate lifts on the wrong language, or waits for a switch that never comes. Gate the app subtree, never the document body — a visitor to a page that already owns its language must not be held on a blank screen waiting for JavaScript it does not need. Use `visibility`, so layout is computed as usual and nothing jumps when it lifts. Always ship the failsafe timer: a page in the wrong language beats an invisible page when a chunk fails to load. Write only to the document element, which already carries the hydration-warning suppression — a pre-hydration write to `<body>` forces that suppression onto `<body>` and permanently silences every other body-level mismatch.
  - *Reference:* `packages/client/src/app/pre-paint.ts:96-160`
- [ ] **Sync the preference, resolve the language per device**
  - The synced preference record stores the preference token (`system` or an explicit locale), never a resolved language. A sync component registers itself as the store's sink, pushes local choices with a pending marker so the server's echo cannot clobber them, and adopts server-side changes. Signed-out screens simply have no sink and keep the choice on the device.
  - *Invariant:* The server must never resolve `system` — it has no device to ask. Storing the resolved language instead of the preference means one account reads the same language on every device, which is exactly what `system` exists to avoid. A push without a pending marker races its own echo and reverts the user's click.
  - *Reference:* `packages/client/src/i18n/locale-sync.tsx:1-61`
- [ ] **Route all user-facing formatting through one module, and keep machine-read output out of it**
  - Pure locale-taking formatters for numbers, fixed decimals, percent, currency, dates in named styles, clock time honouring the user's 12/24-hour preference, weekday names, relative day names, and list joins — with memoised formatter construction and a currency fallback that does not throw. A hook binds them all to the rendering locale. Separately: the tag used for formatting is the device's own tag when its language matches the rendered one, and the bare language otherwise.
  - *Invariant:* Language and region are different axes. A device whose language differs from the rendered one must not contribute its regional conventions, or you get one language's date order under another language's words. And nothing a machine reads back — data exports, the importer, API payloads, error codes, webhook bodies, generated docs, parsed filenames, log lines — ever passes through a locale-aware formatter: a locale-specific decimal separator in an exported cell is a different number to the next program that opens it.
  - *Reference:* `packages/client/src/i18n/format.ts:1-54`
- [ ] **Build public pages once per language as static files, not behind a dynamic locale segment**
  - Each public page lives once as a shared component taking a locale prop; each language gets a one-line re-export under its own path prefix, rendered at build time. Metadata helper emits a per-locale canonical URL, hreflang alternates for every locale plus a default-language `x-default`, and the Open Graph locale plus alternates. A path helper maps every internal link into the current language and supports per-locale slug overrides in both directions. Each page is wrapped in the fixed-locale component so it pins its language and opts out of the pre-paint gate.
  - *Invariant:* A dynamic locale segment at the root competes with every real first-level route and turns unknown paths into localized pages instead of a 404. The served document-level language attribute belongs to the single root layout and cannot differ per prefix without moving every route into groups — so the content wrapper carries the language attribute and the pre-paint script fixes the document element. Never put a date into a message argument on a prerendered page: the build machine's time zone is baked into the HTML and disagrees with hydration.
  - *Reference:* `packages/client/src/i18n/marketing.ts:1-30`
- [ ] **Give the server its own catalog, keyed by generated artifact rather than by screen**
  - A separate catalog and translator in the server package for generated documents, exports and outbound messages, using the same ICU engine, the same exact-key translation type and its own parity test. Default time zone UTC. Missing messages render as the key and never throw.
  - *Invariant:* A generated artifact outlives every session and is re-rendered months later, so its strings are keyed by the artifact, not by a UI screen, and the server must never import a UI-facing catalog. A missing message must not turn a document download into a 500.
  - *Reference:* `packages/server/src/i18n/index.ts:1-73`
- [ ] **Snapshot the language of any artifact handed to an external party**
  - When a record that renders into a document for someone outside the account is created, resolve its language once through an explicit chain (explicit override, then the counterparty's own stored language, then the issuing account's explicit preference, then the default) and store the result on the record. The stored field has no schema default on purpose.
  - *Invariant:* Re-rendering an artifact someone already holds must never change its language. A stored preference of `system` contributes nothing to this chain — the server has no device to ask, and guessing from a request header makes the language of a document depend on which machine happened to press the button. No default on the field means records that predate localization stay in the source language forever, which is the truth about them.
  - *Reference:* `packages/shared/src/locale.ts:69-101`
- [ ] **Resolve outbound message language from the recipient, and render it as a pure function**
  - A service that takes a list of candidate user ids and returns the first explicit preference among them, else the default. Message bodies are pure functions of (locale, arguments) returning subject/text/html, split from the transport, with the language attribute set on the HTML body and every interpolated value escaped.
  - *Invariant:* Only an explicit preference counts; `system` reads as 'not chosen'. The language is the recipient's, never the requester's — whoever typed an address says nothing about who reads the mail. The candidate list exists so a message to an address with no account can fall back to the named sender's language. A lookup failure must yield the default language, never an error: a password reset that does not arrive is far worse than one in the wrong language. Splitting the words from the transport is what makes the translated output unit-testable without a socket or a database.
  - *Reference:* `packages/server/src/services/user-locale.ts:18-44`
- [ ] **Make secondary client surfaces follow the account preference, not the host platform's i18n API**
  - Each additional shipped client (extension, embedded surface, companion app) gets its own catalog using the same engine and typing, resolves the synced account preference with the shared resolver, and keeps a synchronous device-local mirror so its first render is already correct. The host platform's own i18n mechanism is used only where the platform itself renders text — the package manifest's name and description.
  - *Invariant:* A host platform's i18n API answers in the platform's UI language, often fixed at install, and cannot honour an account-level choice — so a user who picked a language in the main app sees a different one in the companion surface with no way to change it. The synchronous mirror matters for surfaces rebuilt on every open: without it the first frame is in the wrong language, every time. Parity for these catalogs must be tested from a package that actually has a test runner.
  - *Reference:* `packages/extension/src/i18n/index.ts:1-30`
- [ ] **Ship the locale picker and a translator-facing glossary**
  - A settings control offering `System` plus each shipped locale, with the pseudo-locale appended in non-production builds. Each language is labelled in its own language in every catalog; only `System` is translated. The change goes through the locale store, not the settings mutation directly. Alongside it, a checked-in glossary per target language: one source concept → exactly one target word across every surface, plus form of address, imperative style and error-message shape.
  - *Invariant:* Language names must be written in their own language, or a reader who landed in the wrong one cannot find their way out. `System` is the exception — it describes the device, not a language. Routing the change through the store gives an instant re-render with no round trip and survives a reload; routing it through the mutation alone does neither. A synonym chosen 'for variety' reads to a user as a different thing, so a missing term goes into the glossary before it goes into a catalog.
  - *Reference:* `packages/client/src/components/settings/language-picker.tsx:24-60`
- [ ] **Take locale-derived values as defaults only where nothing is stored**
  - Conventions a locale implies (week start, and anything similar) are exposed as a per-locale default function and applied only where no stored value exists. A shared/organisation-level setting always wins, whatever language a given member reads in.
  - *Invariant:* If a locale-derived convention overrides a stored shared setting, two members of the same account see different boundaries for the same shared aggregate and their numbers stop reconciling — with no error anywhere. Prefer a small explicit table over a platform API for these: the standard lookup is missing from some browsers and older embedded web views.
  - *Reference:* `packages/shared/src/locale.ts:103-112`
- [ ] **Decide and document which surfaces stay single-language**
  - Where a distribution channel accepts only one language, or the host offers no locale to follow, state that the surface stays in the source language and that shared locale-taking helpers must never be called with a locale from it. Keep per-locale store-listing copy as checked-in files next to the store assets, and give the package manifest an explicit default locale.
  - *Invariant:* A surface that renders in a second language but cannot be published in it is unshippable, and the decision has to be written down or the next contributor localizes it again. Calling a shared locale-taking helper from a single-language surface silently changes output that other consumers depend on being byte-identical.
  - *Reference:* `packages/client/src/i18n/GLOSSARY.de.md:1-6`

### Identifiers this area freezes

Every name below becomes a contract the first time this area ships. All of
them must come from the manifest's `identifiers` block — see
`cli/src/scaffold/identifiers.ts`. An area that spells one of these out as a
literal has re-introduced the divergence that block exists to prevent.

- The locale-preference storage key, `<storagePrefix>.locale` — it lives in every existing browser, native web view and companion surface, and must be resolved from the scaffold-time `storagePrefix` identifier (cli/src/scaffold/identifiers.ts:93-97), never re-derived (reference: packages/client/src/i18n/config.ts:16)
- The pseudo-locale override storage key, `<storagePrefix>.locale.override` (packages/client/src/i18n/config.ts:19)
- The pre-paint gate attribute names — `data-locale-pending` on the document element, `data-locale-gate` on the app subtree, `data-locale-fixed` on subtrees that own their language — because the hand-minified head script, the store, the CSS and the components all hardcode them in different files and languages (packages/client/src/app/pre-paint.ts:150-157, packages/client/src/styles/globals.css:37-42)
- The `data-locale` attribute value written on the document element
- The set of supported locale codes itself: removing one orphans every stored preference that names it (packages/shared/src/locale.ts:15)
- The preference token `system`, stored verbatim in the synced preferences document
- The name of the `locale` field on the synced preferences record and on any snapshotted artifact record
- Catalog namespace names — they are the first segment of every key, appear in the missing-message fallback string, and are shared across client/server/companion catalogs (packages/client/src/i18n/messages/index.ts:34-47)
- URL path prefixes for non-default languages (e.g. `/de/`) and any per-locale slug overrides — these are published, indexed, and referenced by hreflang alternates (packages/client/src/i18n/marketing.ts:47-49)
- The pseudo-locale language tag `en-XA` written into the document language attribute (packages/client/src/i18n/config.ts:31)
- The companion-package manifest's `default_locale` and the `_locales/<code>/messages.json` directory names, plus the `__MSG_*__` key names the manifest references (packages/extension/public/_locales)
- The Open Graph locale strings per language (`en_US`, `de_DE`) once emitted into indexed pages (packages/client/src/i18n/marketing.ts:38)

### Domain-specific — do not extract

- The German glossary's actual term table — account/client/entry/tag word choices, and the specific warning that the English word 'Tag' means 'day' in German (packages/client/src/i18n/GLOSSARY.de.md:24-60). The template should ship the glossary FILE and its one-concept-one-word rule, not these entries.
- Duration formatting helpers and their locale-optional signature — `formatDuration`, `formatDurationShort`, `formatDurationFor`, `formatHours`, `parseDurationInput` accepting comma and dot (packages/client/src/i18n/format.ts:230-262, packages/shared/src/duration.ts). The generic residue is already captured: 'a shared formatter whose output other consumers rely on being byte-identical must default to locale-free'.
- The invoice/e-invoice catalog namespaces and the payment-terms phrasing ('payable within N days'), tax-row wording, and `pdfFormat(locale)` (packages/server/src/services/einvoice/payment-terms.ts:47, CLAUDE.md:1937-1938). The generic mechanism — snapshot an external artifact's language — is extracted as a gap; the invoice-specific vocabulary is not.
- The week-start reasoning stated in terms of timesheets and weekly totals reconciling between members (packages/shared/src/locale.ts:103-112). Extracted generically as 'a shared aggregate's boundary must not vary by reader locale'.
- The `catalog`, `tracker`, `calendar`, `reports`, `einvoice`, `activity`, `members` namespace names themselves — a template ships `common` plus whatever the product's screens are.
- The Raycast-store English-only decision as such (CLAUDE.md:3060-3064). Generalised in the last gap as 'a distribution channel that accepts only one language'; the Raycast specifics are not portable.
- The report-PDF language chain (exporting device's rendered language, else exporter's explicit preference, else default) — CLAUDE.md:3049-3052. I read the rule but did not read the export call site, so I am not asserting a generic form of it beyond the snapshot gap.

### Reference evidence

- Single shared locale contract: supported list, default, preference union, BCP-47 primary-subtag matcher, preference-ordered list matcher, preference resolver, locale-derived week-start default. Header comment states why a second copy anywhere is a bug. — `packages/shared/src/locale.ts:1-112`
- Catalog tooling shared by every package that ships text: `Translation<T>` (exact-key mapped type that widens literals), `flattenMessages`, `mapMessages`, a small ICU walker, `icuPlaceholders`, `pseudoLocalize` with word-sized padding runs — `packages/shared/src/i18n-catalog.ts:1-300`
- Framework-free translator over `use-intl/core`: memoised per locale+namespace, `timeZone()` returns UTC during a build with an explicit warning never to put a date in an ICU argument on a prerendered page, missing message renders `namespace.key` and logs only in dev — `packages/client/src/i18n/translator.ts:1-97`
- `useT(namespace)` — no provider to mount, so existing component tests render unchanged in the source language — `packages/client/src/i18n/use-t.ts:24-25`
- `translate(namespace)` for non-component code, reading the locale at call time; doc comment forbids module-scope use — `packages/client/src/i18n/translate.ts:1-16`
- Locale store with the `target` vs `current` split, lazy `ensureInitialised`, `hydrated` latch, `syncHtml`, `commitTargetLocale`, `releaseLocaleGate`, sink registration, `adoptLocalePreference` (never echoed back), `FixedLocaleContext`, `useSyncExternalStore` with a server snapshot pinned to the prerender language — `packages/client/src/i18n/locale-store.ts:1-307`
- `localeScript(allowPseudo)`: pre-paint resolver duplicated in hand-minified JS, writes `<html lang>`/`data-locale`, sets `data-locale-pending` only when the answer is not the prerender language, 4-second failsafe `setTimeout` removes it, built by a function so the pseudo-locale is absent from production output — `packages/client/src/app/pre-paint.ts:96-160`
- The gate CSS: `visibility` (not `display`/`opacity`) on `[data-locale-gate]`, with `[data-locale-fixed]` setting it back to visible; unlayered so no utility can override it — `packages/client/src/styles/globals.css:29-43`
- `<LocaleRoot>` commits the switch in a layout effect and lifts the gate in the same pre-paint flush; `<FixedLocale>` is a real box, never `display:contents`, because the router's scroll restore skips zero-rect nodes — `packages/client/src/i18n/locale-root.tsx:1-82`
- `<LocaleSync>` — bidirectional preference sync with a `pending` ref so the server's echo does not clobber a local choice; server stores the preference, never the resolved language — `packages/client/src/i18n/locale-sync.tsx:1-61`
- Catalog parity test: same keys, same ICU placeholders/tags, every message parses as ICU in its own locale, and no long source sentence left verbatim in the translation; plus pseudo-locale invariants and plural-rule assertions — `packages/client/src/i18n/catalog-parity.test.ts:1-106`
- Type-level test pinned with `@ts-expect-error` so an unused directive fails `tsc` if the catalog typing ever stops protecting call sites — `packages/client/src/i18n/catalog-types.test.ts:1-45`
- Hydration contract test: renders the prerendered HTML with `renderToString`, hydrates as a non-default-language reader, fails on any console error, asserts the gate is lifted — `packages/client/src/i18n/locale-root.test.tsx:1-107`
- Pre-paint script test that runs the real script string via `new Function`, asserting it marks `<html>` and not `<body>` — because `suppressHydrationWarning` on `<body>` silences every future body-level mismatch, and no e2e assertion can see that prop — `packages/client/src/app/pre-paint.test.ts:1-60`
- Formatting module: `intlLocale()` separates catalog language from device region, memoised `Intl` constructors, named date styles, `formatRelativeDay` via `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat`, `formatList`, currency fallback that does not throw mid-render; header forbids using any of it for machine-read text — `packages/client/src/i18n/format.ts:1-272`
- `useFormat()` binds every pure formatter to the rendering locale — `packages/client/src/i18n/use-format.ts:1-71`
- Catalog index — the only file listing namespaces; both locales bundled statically so a non-default-language first paint never waits on a chunk while the gate holds the page hidden — `packages/client/src/i18n/messages/index.ts:1-67`
- Per-language static public pages: `localizedPath` with a per-locale slug override map, `marketingMetadata` emitting canonical + hreflang alternates + `x-default` + `og:locale`/`alternateLocale`; comment explains why a `[locale]` route segment was rejected and what `/de/` cannot do (`<html lang>` belongs to the one root layout) — `packages/client/src/i18n/marketing.ts:1-107`
- A German route as a one-line re-export of the shared page component with `locale="de"` — `packages/client/src/app/de/privacy/page.tsx:1-15`
- `<MarketingShell>` wraps each public page in `<FixedLocale>` and routes every internal link through `localizedPath` — `packages/client/src/components/marketing/marketing-shell.tsx:41-118`
- Server catalog, separate from the UI catalog, keyed by document rather than by screen; UTC default time zone; missing message never throws — `packages/server/src/i18n/index.ts:1-73`
- Server catalog parity test, mirroring the client's, plus the document-language resolution-order assertions — `packages/server/src/tests/i18n-catalog.test.ts:1-45`
- `explicitLocale` / `preferredLocale`: only an explicit preference counts server-side, a list of candidate users gives a named fallback person, and a lookup failure yields the default language rather than an error — `packages/server/src/services/user-locale.ts:1-44`
- Outbound mail rendered as pure functions (locale + args → subject/text/html), split from the transport so the translated output is unit-testable without a socket; recipient's language, never the request's; `<html lang>` set on the mail body — `packages/server/src/services/transactional-email.ts:1-110`
- Browser extension has its own catalog and resolves the synced account preference with the shared resolver plus a synchronous `localStorage` mirror; explicit rejection of `chrome.i18n`, which follows browser UI language; `public/_locales` holds only the manifest name/description as `__MSG_*__` — `packages/extension/src/i18n/index.ts:1-90`
- Extension catalog parity test run from the client package, because the extension has no test runner of its own — `packages/client/src/i18n/extension-catalog-parity.test.ts:1-59`
- Language picker: language names written in their own language so a reader stranded in the wrong one can escape; only "System" is translated; change goes through the store, not the settings mutation; pseudo-locale never reaches the server — `packages/client/src/components/settings/language-picker.tsx:1-80`
- Translator-facing glossary and voice guide: one concept → one word across every surface, form of address, imperative style, error-message shape — `packages/client/src/i18n/GLOSSARY.de.md:1-60`
- Per-locale store-listing copy kept as a file in the repo alongside the store assets — `docs/marketing/chrome/store-listing.de.md`
- Locale constants importable by server components (no React, no browser APIs); prerender locale, storage keys, pending attribute name, dev-only pseudo flag, `htmlLang`, `shippedLocale` — `packages/client/src/i18n/config.ts:1-39`
